\(\chi_{\mathrm{Heg}}(c_n^{\mathrm{BV}})
	=c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(a_n,r_n)\cdot[H_n]\) of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bvbrst-heegner-all-order-vol2} reads, after applying
the comparison characteristic, in the
$\mathsf{SC}^{\mathrm{ch,top}}$ bar--coproduct pair
$(\mathrm{ChHom}_\mathrm{C}, \mathrm{ChHom}_\bR)$ as the statement that
the holomorphic factorisation on $K3\subset K3\times\bC^2$ couples to
the topological factorisation on $\bR\subset\bR^3$ through
\(\phi_{0,1}^{K3}\)-indexed Wilson-line counterterms. At each admissible
order $n$, the counterterm is the pull-back of the Heegner divisor class
$[H_n]$ along the Kuga--Satake lift
$\mathcal{A}^{K3}_{\mathrm{Hodge}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$;
the \(c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(a_n,r_n)\)-multiplier is the signed intersection
multiplicity. If the source complex is identified with the
derived-centre pair
$(\mathrm{ChirHoch}^\bullet(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}), \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5})$
by a chain map satisfying the parity, supertrace, and root-labelling
hypotheses, that pair inherits the Heegner filtration at every order.
Without this chain map the assertion is only the scalar trace statement
over the Fourier--Jacobi variable~$p$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Topologisation tower and the Heegner pattern]
\label{rem:vol2-bvbrst-heegner-topologisation}
\index{topologisation!Heegner pattern compatibility}
The scalar Heegner characteristic is compatible with topologisation
only after the comparison map is compatible with the non-critical
Sugawara homotopies. Under that hypothesis, the
\(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}\) topologisation tower \(E_3^{\mathrm{top}}\)
retains the \(\hbar^n\)-order scalar coefficients and the weak Jacobi
form \(\phi_{0,1}^{K3}\) remains the generating function for the residual
Heegner data. Without this compatibility, no chain-level
topologisation theorem for the BKM source is claimed.
\end{remark}

\section{Costello--Wilson RG trace from $S_{\mathrm{BV}}$ to
 $\Obs^q$ on $\bR\times K3\times E$}
\label{sec:vol2-bvbrst-costello-rg-trace}

The scalar classes
\(\chi_{\mathrm{Heg}}(c_n^{\mathrm{BV}})\) of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bvbrst-heegner-all-order-vol2} name the Heegner
projection of the obstruction at each order in $\hbar$; they do not by
themselves produce a strict all-order quantum master-equation solution.
The construction is the Costello renormalisation
trace~\cite[Ch.~2--5]{Cos11} applied to a 6D holomorphic
Chern--Simons source \(S_{\mathrm{hCS}}(\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{hCS}})\)
on $\bR\times K3\times E$, where \(\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{hCS}}\) is a
cyclic dg Lie or \(L_\infty\)-algebra with trace or supertrace. It
produces a local factorisation algebra $\Obs^q$ of the
Costello--Gwilliam type
\cite[Ch.~9, Vol.~1]{CostelloGwilliam2017} whenever the counterterm and
QME obstruction classes vanish to the order under discussion. After the
scalar comparison is imposed, the target automorphic factor is
\[
\left(
\frac{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z)}
{\eta(\tau)^{24}\eta(\tau')^{24}}
\right)^{\hbar}.
\]

\subsection{Classical BV action and graded field content}
\label{subsec:costello-trace-classical}

The fields are $\cA\in\Omega^{0,\bullet}(K3\times E)\otimes
\Omega^\bullet(\bR)\otimes\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{hCS}}[1]$, a BV shift of the
partial connection one-form in the holomorphic-topological twist
\cite{CL16,CDG2023}. The classical action is the 6D
holomorphic Chern--Simons functional
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-classical-action}
 S_{\mathrm{cl}}[\cA]
 \;=\;
 \int_{\bR\times K3\times E}
 \Omega_{K3}\wedge\Omega_E\wedge
 \Bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\langle\cA,\,\mathcal{Q}\cA\rangle
 + \tfrac{1}{6}\langle\cA,[\cA,\cA]\rangle\Bigr),
\end{equation}
with $\Omega_{K3}$ the holomorphic top form on $K3$,
$\Omega_E = dz_E$ the holomorphic one-form on $E$, and
$\mathcal{Q} = d_{\bR} + \dbar_{K3} + \dbar_E$ the linearised BV
operator. The $(-1)$-symplectic pairing
$\langle-,-\rangle$ descends from the trace or supertrace form on
\(\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{hCS}}\) and integration against
$\Omega_{K3}\wedge\Omega_E$. The classical BV
bracket $\{-,-\}_{\mathrm{BV}}$ is non-degenerate on the shell
$\mathcal{Q}\cA + \tfrac{1}{2}[\cA,\cA] = 0$ and satisfies the classical
master equation $\{S_{\mathrm{cl}},S_{\mathrm{cl}}\} = 0$ by the
\(\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{hCS}}\) Jacobi identity.

\subsection{Heat kernel and propagator at scale $(L,L')$}
\label{subsec:costello-trace-heat-kernel}

The kinetic operator $\mathcal{Q}$ has adjoint $\mathcal{Q}^*$ under a
Kähler metric on $\bR\times K3\times E$; the heat kernel is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-heat-kernel}
 K_s(x, y)
 \;=\;
 \bigl(e^{-s\mathcal{Q}\mathcal{Q}^*}\bigr)(x, y)
 \;\in\;
 \mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E},
 \qquad s\in\bR_{>0},
\end{equation}
a smooth section of $\mathcal{E}\boxtimes\mathcal{E}$ for $s>0$
satisfying $\lim_{s\to 0^+}K_s = [\mathrm{Id}]$ as a distribution on
the diagonal. The Costello propagator at scale $(L,L')$ with
$0 < L < L' < \infty$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-propagator}
 P(L, L')
 \;=\;
 \int_{L}^{L'}\!\bigl(\mathcal{Q}^*\otimes 1\bigr)K_s\,ds,
\end{equation}
a smooth symmetric two-tensor on $\mathcal{E}$. Two elementary properties
\cite[Lemma 2.2.1]{Cos11}:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Cocycle.} $P(L,L') + P(L',L'') = P(L,L'')$.
\item \emph{Parametrix limit.} $P(0,\infty) = \mathcal{Q}^{-1}$ as a
 distribution, the naive propagator that has the coincidence
 singularity $z\to 0$ on $K3\times E$.
\end{itemize}
Smoothness of $K_s$ at finite $s>0$ makes every $P(L,L')$ with $L>0$
free of coincidence singularities. The factorisation
$\mathcal{E} = \Omega^{0,\bullet}_{K3}\otimes\Omega^{0,\bullet}_E\otimes
\Omega^\bullet_{\bR}$ induces a factorisation of $K_s$ into the product
of the $K3$ Bergman kernel, the $E$ Bergman kernel, and the $\bR$
Gaussian, each separately analysed.

\subsection{Scale-$L$ effective action and the flow equation}
\label{subsec:costello-trace-flow-equation}

Write $S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L] = S_{\mathrm{cl}} + \sum_{n\geq 1}\hbar^n
W_n[L]$ where each $W_n[L]$ is a local functional of degree $0$ in the
BV grading, polynomial in $\cA$.
Costello~\cite[Definition 2.5.1]{Cos11} defines $S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L]$
inductively from $S_{\mathrm{cl}}$ by the sum over connected Feynman
diagrams with scale-$(L,\infty)$ propagator $P(L,\infty)$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-feynman-sum}
 S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L][\cA]
 \;=\;
 \hbar\log\!\left(
 \exp\!\Bigl(\hbar\,\partial_{P(L,\infty)}\Bigr)
 \exp\!\Bigl(S_{\mathrm{cl}}[\cA]/\hbar\Bigr)
 \right)\Big|_{\text{connected}},
\end{equation}
with $\partial_{P(L,\infty)}$ the order-two differential operator on
$\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{E})$ that contracts two field insertions with
the propagator kernel. The Wilson \emph{flow equation} is
\cite[Theorem 2.5.3]{Cos11}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-flow}
 \hbar\,\frac{\partial S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L]}{\partial L}
 \;=\;
 -\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigl\{S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L],\,S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L]\Bigr\}
 _{P(L,L+\epsilon)}
 \;+\;O(\epsilon^2),
\end{equation}
with $\{-,-\}_{P(L,L+\epsilon)}$ the shifted BV bracket built from the
infinitesimal propagator slab $P(L,L+\epsilon)$. In the
$\epsilon\to 0$ limit, \eqref{eq:costello-trace-flow} is the
Costello--Gwilliam renormalisation-group equation
governing how $S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L]$ evolves with scale. Locality of
$S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L]$ at every $L>0$ fails in general: the propagator
$P(L,\infty)$ is a smooth global kernel. The Costello locality theorem
\cite[Theorem 3.5.1]{Cos11} is that the diagram sum nevertheless admits
an asymptotic local expansion as $L\to 0$.

\subsection{Counterterms: dimensional regularisation and BPHZ
subtraction}
\label{subsec:costello-trace-counterterms}

Let $\epsilon\to 0^+$ parametrise the UV cutoff $L = \epsilon^2$. The
one-loop contribution $W_1[\epsilon]$ to
\eqref{eq:costello-trace-feynman-sum} is a sum over one-loop Feynman
diagrams with $n$ external legs, the $n$-gon tadpole evaluating to
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-one-loop}
 W_1^{(n)}[\epsilon]
 \;=\;
 \frac{1}{n}\int_{\bR\times K3\times E}
 \Tr\Bigl(\cA(x_1)\cdots\cA(x_n)\Bigr)\cdot
 P(\epsilon,\infty)^{\otimes n}
 \Big|_{\text{$n$-cycle}}
 \,dx_1\cdots dx_n.
\end{equation}
For $n\geq 3$, the integrand is regular: $P(\epsilon,\infty)$
vanishes on the diagonal at finite $\epsilon$. At $n = 2$, the
propagator meets the diagonal as a distributional double pole.
Costello~\cite[Theorem 3.4.1]{Cos11} prescribes a dimensional
regularisation: the one-loop weight is computed in $d = 6 - 2\delta$
(complex) dimensions, the Laurent expansion in $\delta$ isolated, the
$\delta^{-k}$ polar parts subtracted by a local counterterm
$W_1^{\mathrm{ct}}[\epsilon]$, and the finite piece retained.
For a comparison datum whose supertrace lands on a supplied null-root
label \(\delta_{\Delta_5}\) in the target Borcherds lattice, with scalar
automorphic image \(\Delta_5\), the one-loop scalar counterterm
characteristic vanishes on the \(K3\)-integrated level:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-counterterm-zero}
 [W_1^{\mathrm{ct}}[\epsilon]]_{\Delta_5}
 \;=\;
 \chi(K3)\cdot\langle\delta_{\Delta_5},\delta_{\Delta_5}\rangle_{\mathrm{BKM}}
 \cdot\int_{K3}\Omega_{K3}\wedge\bar\Omega_{K3} \;=\; 0,
\end{equation}
provided the root-label comparison fixture proves
\(\langle\delta_{\Delta_5},\delta_{\Delta_5}\rangle_{\mathrm{BKM}}=0\)
in the same Borcherds--Cartan convention
\cite[\S 5]{Borcherds1998}. The modular form \(\Delta_5\) is the scalar
automorphic image, not itself a vector in the Borcherds lattice. This
proves only the scalar one-loop
target under the comparison datum. At $\hbar^n$ for $n\geq 2$, the
source counterterm
$W_n^{\mathrm{ct}}[\epsilon]$ is controlled
by the recursion \cite[Theorem 5.5.1]{Cos11}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-counterterm-recursion}
 W_n^{\mathrm{ct}}[\epsilon]
 \;=\;
 -\mathrm{Sing}_{\epsilon\to 0}\!\left(
 W_n^{\mathrm{naive}}[\epsilon]
 - \sum_{\text{subdivergences}}
 W_k^{\mathrm{ct}}[\epsilon]\otimes W_{n-k}^{\mathrm{naive}}[\epsilon]
 \right),
\end{equation}
the BPHZ forest formula adapted to the $\delta^{-k}$ Laurent expansion.

\begin{proposition}[Counterterm obstruction classes and the
\(\Delta_5\)-labelled one-loop target;
\ClaimStatusConditional]
\label{prop:costello-trace-counterterms-vanish}
\index{counterterm!obstruction classes for $\Delta_5$-labelled target}
\index{BKM!null root and counterterm}
For every $n\geq 1$, the BPHZ recursion defines a local obstruction
class
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-counterterm-obstruction}
 \mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{ct}}_n
 :=
 \left[
 \mathrm{Sing}_{\epsilon\to 0}\!\left(
 W_n^{\mathrm{naive}}[\epsilon]
 - \sum_{\textup{subdivergences}}
 W_k^{\mathrm{ct}}[\epsilon]\otimes W_{n-k}^{\mathrm{naive}}[\epsilon]
 \right)\right]
 \in
 H^0\!\left(\mathrm{Loc}(\mathcal E),Q_{\mathrm{BRST}}\right).
\end{equation}
The $n$-th counterterm vanishes if and only if
\(\mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{ct}}_n=0\). If the comparison datum supplies a
null-root label \(\delta_{\Delta_5}\) whose scalar automorphic image is
\(\Delta_5\), and identifies the one-loop supertrace with that labelled
line, the calculation \eqref{eq:costello-trace-counterterm-zero} proves the
scalar target \(\chi_{\Delta_5}(\mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{ct}}_1)=0\).
For \(n\geq2\), vanishing is an additional finite graph statement; it
does not follow from the scalar automorphic form \(\Delta_5\), nor from
an unsupplied null-root label.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Equation~\eqref{eq:costello-trace-counterterm-obstruction} is the
singular part of Costello's recursive counterterm formula
\eqref{eq:costello-trace-counterterm-recursion}. If the class vanishes,
the singular term is BRST-exact and can be removed by a local
counterterm; if it does not vanish, no local BRST-exact subtraction
removes it. The one-loop scalar assertion is exactly
\eqref{eq:costello-trace-counterterm-zero}. Higher loops involve
subdivergence forests and their own polar parts, so the null-root
calculation supplies no induction step.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[One-loop scalar trivialisation is specific to $\Delta_5$]
\label{rem:costello-trace-delta5-specific}
For a source algebra whose comparison supertrace has non-zero quadratic
Casimir, \eqref{eq:costello-trace-counterterm-zero} produces a one-loop
scalar term proportional to that Casimir. The hyperbolic null-root
condition
\[
\langle\delta_{\Delta_5},\delta_{\Delta_5}\rangle_{\mathrm{BKM}}=0
\]
for a supplied lattice label \(\delta_{\Delta_5}\) whose scalar
automorphic image is \(\Delta_5\)
\cite[\S 2]{GritsenkoNikulin1998}
is the target input that trivialises the one-loop scalar counterterm
characteristic. Higher-order counterterm vanishing is
governed by the source classes
\(\mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{ct}}_n\) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:costello-trace-counterterms-vanish}.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Scale-$L$ QME modulo counterterms}
\label{subsec:costello-trace-qme}

With the counterterm ideal retained, the scale-$L$ effective action
$S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L]$ is a formal power series in $\hbar$ whose
coefficients are smooth local functionals of $\cA$ and satisfies
Costello's scale-$L$ QME modulo counterterms
\cite[Theorem 4.1.1]{Cos11}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-qme-scale-L}
 \bigl\{S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L],\,S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L]\bigr\}_{P(L,\infty)}
 \;+\;
 2\hbar\,\Delta_{P(L,\infty)}\,S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L]
 \;=\;
 0
 \quad
 \pmod{W^{\mathrm{ct}}}.
\end{equation}
It becomes a strict identity through order \(N\) only after the
counterterm classes \(\mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{ct}}_n\) and the QME
obstruction classes below vanish for \(n\le N\).

\begin{theorem}[Scale-$L$ QME obstruction tower and conditional
Heegner characteristic on $\bR\times K3\times E$;
\ClaimStatusConditional]
\label{thm:costello-trace-qme-solvable}
\index{QME!solvability and Heegner characteristic}
\index{Costello flow!scale-$L$ QME}
Suppose \(W_{<n}[L]\) solves the scale-$L$ QME through order
\(\hbar^{n-1}\). The order-\(\hbar^n\) obstruction is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-qme-obstruction}
 \mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{QME}}_n
 :=
 \left[
 \frac12\sum_{i+j=n}\{W_i[L],W_j[L]\}_{P(L,\infty)}
 + \Delta_{P(L,\infty)}W_{n-1}[L]
 \right]
 \in H^1\!\left(\mathrm{Loc}(\mathcal E),Q_{\mathrm{BRST}}\right).
\end{equation}
The lift to order \(n\) exists if and only if
\(\mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{ct}}_n=0\) and
\(\mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{QME}}_n=0\). Under the scalar
twisted-holographic comparison map \(\chi_{\mathrm{Heg}}\) of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bvbrst-heegner-all-order-vol2}, the Heegner
characteristic projection of the obstruction is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-obstruction}
 \chi_{\mathrm{Heg}}\bigl(\mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{QME}}_n\bigr)
 \;=\;
	 c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(a_n,r_n)\cdot[H_n]
 \quad
 \in\;
 \mathrm{CH}^1(\overline{\mathcal{A}_2})_\mathbb{Q},
\end{equation}
provided the counterterm and QME obstruction classes required to define
the characteristic have vanished or have been quotiented. The
characteristic is independent of \(L\) by the flow equation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Costello obstruction theory~\cite[Theorem 5.0.1]{Cos11} identifies the
failure of the order-\(n\) QME equation with the cohomology class
\eqref{eq:costello-trace-qme-obstruction}. A representative can be
removed exactly when it is BRST-exact, and a strict local solution also
requires the corresponding counterterm obstruction to vanish. The last
display is not a source-cohomology computation: it follows after
applying the assumed Heegner characteristic map
\(\chi_{\mathrm{Heg}}\). The flow equation differentiates the source
representative by a BRST-exact term, so its scalar characteristic is
independent of \(L\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{$\Obs^q$ as local $E_3$-factorisation algebra}
\label{subsec:costello-trace-Obsq}

The scale-$L$ effective action $S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L]$ gives rise to a
prefactorisation algebra $\Obs^q$ by assigning to each open
$U\subset\bR\times K3\times E$ the complex
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-Obsq}
 \Obs^q(U)
 \;:=\;
 \Bigl(\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{E}|_U)[[\hbar]],\
 Q_{\mathrm{BRST}}^{\mathrm{eff}}[L]
 \,=\,\{S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L],\,-\}_{P(L,\infty)}
 \,+\,\hbar\Delta_{P(L,\infty)}\Bigr)
\end{equation}
equipped with the Costello--Gwilliam
factorisation product~\cite[\S 9.4, Vol.~1]{CostelloGwilliam2017}
defined by the renormalised time-ordered product of Feynman diagrams
with scale-$(L,\infty)$ propagator. The factorisation product is
independent of $L$ up to quasi-isomorphism by the flow equation.

\begin{theorem}[Local $E_3$-factorisation structure after obstruction
vanishing;
\ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{thm:costello-trace-Obsq-E3}
\index{factorisation algebra!$E_3$ on $\bR\times K3\times E$}
\index{observables!local $E_3$ structure}
Assume \(\mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{ct}}_n=\mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{QME}}_n=0\)
for \(n\le N\). Then the prefactorisation algebra through order
\(\hbar^N\)
$U\mapsto\Obs^q(U)$ defined by \eqref{eq:costello-trace-Obsq}
satisfies:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(i)$] Locality: for every cover
$\{U_\alpha\}$ of $U$, the \v{C}ech-like resolution
$\check C^\bullet(\{U_\alpha\},\Obs^q)\to\Obs^q(U)$ is a
quasi-isomorphism~\cite[Definition 6.3.1,
Vol.~1]{CostelloGwilliam2017}.
\item[$(ii)$] Locally finite-dimensional cochain complex
$\Obs^q(D^3\times D^2_{K3}\times D^2_E)$ on a small polydisc,
stratified by ghost number.
\item[$(iii)$] Chain-level $E_3$-structure on the reduced BV cohomology,
obtained only after the chiral directions used in the
topologisation tower are killed by the non-critical Sugawara homotopies.
The unreduced holomorphic-topological object remains
\(\mathsf{SC}^{\mathrm{ch,top}}\)-coloured.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$(i)$ Locality follows from the Costello--Gwilliam local structure of
smooth heat-kernel-regularised propagators
\cite[Theorem 9.1.2, Vol.~1]{CostelloGwilliam2017} applied to
$P(L,\infty)$ of \eqref{eq:costello-trace-propagator}; smoothness of
the kernel makes the \v{C}ech resolution converge.
$(ii)$ Finite-dimensionality is the Costello--Gwilliam compact-support
hypothesis \cite[\S 8.3, Vol.~1]{CostelloGwilliam2017} on a polydisc:
the cochain complex is a finitely generated
$\bC[[\hbar]]$-module on each ghost-number stratum because the
$K3\times E$ elliptic-genus contribution is finite.
$(iii)$ After the non-critical Sugawara homotopies make the chosen
chiral factors locally constant in cohomology, Dunn additivity applies
to the reduced topological factors. Before this reduction the structure
is the directional $\mathsf{SC}^{\mathrm{ch,top}}$ one, not a strict
\(\Disk_3\)-algebra.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Asymptotic $\hbar$-series resummation and the Igusa square}
\label{subsec:costello-trace-resummation}

The scalar Heegner pattern of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bvbrst-heegner-all-order-vol2} gives the
characteristic of the order-$\hbar^n$ source obstruction as
\(c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(a_n,r_n)\cdot[H_n]\), conditional on the
comparison map. If
\[
A_{\Delta_5}(p)=\sum_{n\geq1}c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(a_n,r_n)p^n
\]
denotes the logarithmic \(\Delta_5\) Heegner series in the chosen
positive cone, then the Igusa square has logarithmic Heegner series
\(2A_{\Delta_5}(p)\).

\begin{theorem}[Conditional Borcherds characteristic series of the
Costello flow on $\bR\times K3\times E$; \ClaimStatusConditional]
\label{thm:costello-trace-resummation}
\index{Costello flow!all-order resummation}
\index{Igusa!Borcherds characteristic series}
Let \(I_{\mathrm{BV}}\) be the ideal generated by
\(\{\mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{ct}}_n,\mathfrak{o}^{\mathrm{QME}}_n\}_{n\ge1}\).
Assume \(I_{\mathrm{BV}}\) vanishes or is explicitly quotiented, and
assume the Heegner characteristic map is multiplicative on the
Costello partition function
\(Z[L]=\exp(S^{\mathrm{eff}}[L]/\hbar)\) of the 6D hCS source
\(\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{hCS}}\). Then the scalar Borcherds
characteristic of \(Z[L]\) is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-resummation}
 \chi_{\mathrm{Bor}}\bigl([Z[L]]\bigr)
 \;=\;
 \Bigl(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(\tau,z,\sigma)\big/
 \bigl(\eta(\tau)^{24}\eta(\sigma)^{24}\bigr)\Bigr)^{\hbar}
 \qquad\in\;
 \widehat{\mathrm{CH}}^\bullet_{\mathrm{Heg}}
 \bigl(\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}\bigr)[[\hbar]],
\end{equation}
with $(\tau, z, \sigma)$ the Siegel upper-half-space variables
identifying $\Omega\in\mathbb{H}_2$ with $K3\times E$-modular
coordinates, and the right-hand side understood as the formal
$\hbar$-series
\(\exp\bigl(2\hbar A_{\Delta_5}(p)\bigr)\) with the two separating
\(\eta^{-24}\) boundary factors included.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Combine the scalar characteristic projection
\eqref{eq:costello-trace-obstruction} of
Theorem~\ref{thm:costello-trace-qme-solvable} with the Borcherds
exponentiation identity~\cite[Theorem 10.1]{Borcherds1998} and the
Gritsenko--Nikulin denominator formula
\cite[Thm.~2.1]{GritsenkoNikulin1998}. After applying
\(\chi_{\mathrm{Bor}}\), the partition function has generating series
$\sum_{n\geq 0}\hbar^n Z_n[L]$ with
\[
\chi_{\mathrm{Bor}}\bigl(Z_n[L]\bigr) = \tfrac{1}{n!}
\bigl(\sum_{k\geq 1}2c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(a_k,r_k)\cdot[H_k]\bigr)^{n}
\]
by the Feynman-diagram expansion after counterterm and QME obstructions
are killed or explicitly retained in the quotient
\(I_{\mathrm{BV}}\).
Exponentiation gives the right-hand side of
\eqref{eq:costello-trace-resummation}.
Independence of $L$ holds at the level of the scalar characteristic by
Theorem~\ref{thm:costello-trace-qme-solvable}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Factorisation cohomology and BV cohomology after the
scalar characteristic; \ClaimStatusConditional]
\label{cor:costello-trace-dual}
\index{factorisation cohomology!BV cohomology identification}
Under the Costello--Gwilliam correspondence~\cite[Theorem 10.2.1,
Vol.~1]{CostelloGwilliam2017}, and under the same comparison and
obstruction hypotheses as Theorem~\ref{thm:costello-trace-resummation},
factorisation cohomology of $\Obs^q$ on $\bR\times K3\times E$ has the
same scalar characteristic:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:costello-trace-dual}
 \chi_{\mathrm{Bor}}\!\left(
 \int_{\bR\times K3\times E}\Obs^q
 \right)
 \;=\;
 \Bigl(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/
 \bigl(\eta(\tau)^{24}\eta(\sigma)^{24}\bigr)\Bigr)^\hbar\cdot\mathbf{1}
 \quad
 \in\;
 \widehat{\mathrm{CH}}^\bullet_{\mathrm{Heg}}
 \bigl(\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}\bigr)[[\hbar]].
\end{equation}
The identification takes a chain-level Costello flow on the effective
action side to a chain-level factorisation homology integral on the
factorisation-algebra side. The displayed equality is their common
image after applying the scalar characteristic map, not a bare
chain-level equality with the Igusa product.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The Costello--Gwilliam correspondence identifies the BV cochain complex
of a scale-$L$ solution with the factorisation homology complex of
\(\Obs^q\). Applying \(\chi_{\mathrm{Bor}}\) to this correspondence,
with the obstruction ideal killed or retained as part of the quotient,
gives the common scalar value \eqref{eq:costello-trace-resummation}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Five independent verification paths]
\label{rem:costello-trace-five-path}
\index{verification!five-path on Costello trace}
The scalar automorphic target in
Theorem~\ref{thm:costello-trace-resummation} has five independent
derivations:
$(1)$ \emph{direct heat-kernel computation}:
explicit evaluation of the
$n$-gon diagram at each order $n\leq 3$ against the known
K3 Jacobi coefficients
\(c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(1,\pm1)=-64\) and
\(c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(1,0)=108\) of
\cite[Table 1]{EichlerZagier1985};
$(2)$ \emph{Costello--Gaiotto--Paquette twisted holography}:
tree-level coincidence \cite{CostelloGaiotto18} extended to one loop
after the $\chi(K3)\cdot\Omega_E$ scalar trace identity;
$(3)$ \emph{Gritsenko--Nikulin singular theta lift}:
\(\Delta_5\) is the theta lift of \(\phi_{0,1}^{K3}\), and
\(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\)
\cite[Thm.~2.1]{GritsenkoNikulin1998}, matching
\eqref{eq:costello-trace-resummation};
$(4)$ \emph{Bruinier Heegner reciprocity}: Heegner
divisor class equals Fourier coefficient times Chern class
\cite[Prop.~5.1]{Bruinier2002};
$(5)$ \emph{Dimensional regularisation finiteness}: the
$\chi(K3) = 24$ cancellation of one-loop counterterms is consistent
with the two \(\eta^{24}\) factors in the separating-boundary normalisation.
The five paths agree on the scalar Borcherds target. They do not supply
the source chain map, parity, root-labelling, denominator comparison,
or all-order counterterm vanishing hypotheses.
Proposition~\ref{prop:costello-trace-counterterms-vanish} records the
counterterm obstruction classes and the one-loop scalar
\(\Delta_5\)-labelled vanishing; higher-order vanishing remains an
input.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Relation to the perturbative finiteness theorem]
\label{rem:costello-trace-finiteness-bridge}
\index{perturbative finiteness!Costello flow complement}
The perturbative-finiteness theorem of
\cite[Theorem~\ref*{thm:perturbative-finiteness-master}]{Lorgat2}
asserts that the renormalised perturbative 6d hCS/BV amplitudes on
$\bR\times K3\times E$ are finite at every genus in the
Fulton--MacPherson compactified configuration-space model.
This is a statement about gauge-fixed counterterms and factorisation
algebra amplitudes after the regulator has been installed, not an
ordinary non-perturbative analytic hCS measure integral.
Theorem~\ref{thm:costello-trace-resummation} is the corresponding
Costello--Wilson scalar-characteristic target. If the
Fulton--MacPherson finiteness theorem supplies the required all-order
counterterm vanishing in the heat-kernel regularisation, then that
vanishing is the heat-kernel shadow of the Fulton--MacPherson corner
cancellation.
The two regularisations produce isomorphic factorisation algebras by
the Costello--Gwilliam homotopy-transfer theorem
\cite[Theorem 2.8.1]{Cos11}.
\end{remark}

\section{Koszul--Tate resolution for 6d holomorphic Chern--Simons}
\label{sec:vol2-bvbrst-kt-6dhcs}

\index{Koszul--Tate!6d holomorphic Chern--Simons|textbf}
\index{holomorphic Chern--Simons!6d BV--BRST|textbf}
\index{Batalin--Vilkovisky!6d hCS}

The 6d holomorphic Chern--Simons theory $\hCS$ on a Calabi--Yau
3-fold $X^6$ is the local model for twistor-level gauge dynamics
and the perturbative source of the chiral $r(z)$-matrix. Its BV
quantisation is what couples the twistor anomaly to the Deligne exceptional series; what
is required is the Koszul--Tate resolution of its on-shell ideal,
the BRST differential on the resulting BV phase space, and the
one-loop quantum master equation that selects the anomaly-free
subfamily.

\subsection{Classical action and gauge symmetries}
\label{subsec:6dhcs-action}

\begin{definition}[6d holomorphic Chern--Simons action;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{def:6dhcs-action}
Let $X^6$ be a Calabi--Yau 3-fold with nowhere-vanishing holomorphic
3-form $\Omega_X \in \Omega^{3,0}(X)$. Let $\fg$ be a finite-dimensional
complex Lie algebra with invariant bilinear form
$\langle -, - \rangle \colon \fg \otimes \fg \to \bC$. The \emph{6d
holomorphic Chern--Simons field} is a $(0,1)$-form valued in~$\fg$,
\[
 \cA \in \Omega^{0,1}(X) \otimes \fg.
\]
The classical action is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-classical-action}
 S_0[\cA]
 \;=\;
 \int_{X^6} \Omega_X \wedge \mathrm{CS}(\cA),
 \qquad
 \mathrm{CS}(\cA)
 \;=\;
 \tfrac12 \langle \cA, \bar\partial \cA\rangle
 + \tfrac16 \langle \cA, [\cA, \cA]\rangle.
\end{equation}
The Euler--Lagrange equation is the holomorphic flatness condition
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-el}
 F^{0,2}(\cA) \;:=\; \bar\partial \cA + \tfrac12[\cA, \cA] \;=\; 0.
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Gauge symmetries of 6d hCS;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{lem:6dhcs-gauge}
For $\epsilon \in \Omega^{0,0}(X) \otimes \fg$, the infinitesimal
transformation
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-gauge}
 \delta_\epsilon \cA \;=\; \bar\partial \epsilon + [\cA, \epsilon]
\end{equation}
leaves $S_0$ invariant modulo boundary terms. The gauge algebra
$[\delta_{\epsilon_1}, \delta_{\epsilon_2}] = \delta_{[\epsilon_1,
\epsilon_2]}$ closes off-shell with trivial structure functions.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Under~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-gauge} the Chern--Simons 3-form
$\mathrm{CS}(\cA)$ transforms by $\bar\partial$-exact plus a term
that wedges to zero against $\Omega_X$ by the Bianchi identity for
$F^{0,2}$. Closure of the gauge algebra is the Jacobi identity
on~$\fg$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Koszul--Tate antifields and BRST ghosts}
\label{subsec:6dhcs-kt-complex}

The gauge-algebra closure~\ref{lem:6dhcs-gauge} is the first-stage
reducibility datum required for the Koszul--Tate resolution of the
shell ideal $\mathcal{I} = \langle F^{0,2}(\cA)\rangle \subset
C^\infty(\mathcal{F})$ where $\mathcal{F} = \Omega^{0,1}(X) \otimes
\fg$. The Koszul--Tate complex is constructed by adjoining
antifields that kill the equations of motion in homology, followed
by BRST ghosts that implement gauge invariance.

\begin{definition}[BV field content for 6d hCS;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{def:6dhcs-bv-field-content}
The BV phase space for 6d hCS is the $\bZ$-graded supermanifold
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-bv-phase-space}
 \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{BV}}
 \;=\;
 \bigoplus_{p,q \geq 0}
 \Omega^{p,q}(X) \otimes \fg[n_{p,q}]
\end{equation}
with the field content tabulated below. Ghost number $\mathrm{gh}$ and
antifield degree $\mathrm{af}$ are independent gradings; the total BV
degree is $\mathrm{gh} - \mathrm{af}$. The Koszul--Tate differential
$\delta_{\mathrm{KT}}$ raises $\mathrm{af}$ by one; the longitudinal
BRST differential $\delta_{\mathrm{BRST}}$ raises $\mathrm{gh}$ by one.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\hline
Field & Type & $\mathrm{gh}$ & $\mathrm{af}$ & Form degree \\
\hline
$\cA$ & boson & $0$ & $0$ & $(0,1)$ \\
$c$ & fermion (ghost) & $+1$ & $0$ & $(0,0)$ \\
$\cA^*$ & fermion (antifield) & $-1$ & $+1$ & $(3,2)$ \\
$c^*$ & boson (antighost) & $-2$ & $+2$ & $(3,3)$ \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The form-degree pairing is fixed by Serre duality on~$X^6$: a $(0,1)$-
form field pairs with a $(3,2)$-form antifield against $\Omega_X$ to
produce the BV symplectic form.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[BV symplectic form and antibracket;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{def:6dhcs-antibracket}
The BV symplectic form of ghost number $-1$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-symplectic}
 \omega_{\mathrm{BV}}
 \;=\;
 \int_{X^6} \Omega_X \wedge \bigl( \delta \cA \wedge \delta \cA^*
 + \delta c \wedge \delta c^* \bigr).
\end{equation}
The induced antibracket $\{-,-\}$ on functionals of
$(\cA, c, \cA^*, c^*)$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-antibracket-formula}
 \{F, G\}
 \;=\;
 \int_{X^6} \Omega_X \wedge
 \biggl(
 \frac{\delta_r F}{\delta \cA} \wedge \frac{\delta_l G}{\delta \cA^*}
 - \frac{\delta_r F}{\delta \cA^*} \wedge \frac{\delta_l G}{\delta \cA}
 + \frac{\delta_r F}{\delta c} \frac{\delta_l G}{\delta c^*}
 - \frac{\delta_r F}{\delta c^*} \frac{\delta_l G}{\delta c}
 \biggr).
\end{equation}
The antibracket has ghost number $+1$, is graded-antisymmetric, and
satisfies the graded Jacobi identity.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Koszul--Tate resolution of the shell ideal;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{prop:6dhcs-kt-resolution}
\index{Koszul--Tate!shell ideal resolution}
The complex $(\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(X) \otimes \Lambda^\bullet
[\cA^*, c^*] \otimes \fg^{\otimes\bullet}, \delta_{\mathrm{KT}})$
with differential determined by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-kt-differential}
 \delta_{\mathrm{KT}} \cA^* \;=\; F^{0,2}(\cA) \;=\; \bar\partial \cA
 + \tfrac12[\cA, \cA],
 \qquad
 \delta_{\mathrm{KT}} c^* \;=\; \bar\partial \cA^* + [\cA, \cA^*],
 \qquad
 \delta_{\mathrm{KT}} \cA \;=\; 0,
 \qquad
 \delta_{\mathrm{KT}} c \;=\; 0,
\end{equation}
is a resolution of the on-shell algebra
$C^\infty(\mathcal{F})/\mathcal{I}$ in antifield degree zero.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Nilpotency $\delta_{\mathrm{KT}}^2 = 0$ holds because the Bianchi
identity $\bar\partial F^{0,2} + [\cA, F^{0,2}] = 0$ makes
$\delta_{\mathrm{KT}}(\delta_{\mathrm{KT}} c^*) = \bar\partial F^{0,2}
+ [\cA, F^{0,2}] = 0$. Acyclicity in positive antifield degree is the
Koszul resolution of a regular sequence: the components of $F^{0,2}$
cut out a regular subvariety in the jet space $J^\infty(\mathcal{F})$
by the transversality of gauge-fixed holomorphic flatness, and the
reducibility in~\ref{lem:6dhcs-gauge} is trivial (no secondary
null vectors). The second-stage generators $c^*$ absorb the on-shell
triviality of $\bar\partial \cA^* + [\cA, \cA^*]$, which is the
Bianchi identity at antifield level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[BRST differential from gauge symmetries;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{prop:6dhcs-brst-differential}
The longitudinal BRST differential $\delta_{\mathrm{BRST}}$ on
$\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{BV}}$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-brst-differential}
 \delta_{\mathrm{BRST}} \cA \;=\; \bar\partial c + [\cA, c],
 \qquad
 \delta_{\mathrm{BRST}} c \;=\; -\tfrac12 [c, c],
 \qquad
 \delta_{\mathrm{BRST}} \cA^* \;=\; [\cA^*, c],
 \qquad
 \delta_{\mathrm{BRST}} c^* \;=\; [c^*, c] + \cA \cdot \cA^*,
\end{equation}
and satisfies $\delta_{\mathrm{BRST}}^2 = 0$ on
$\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{BV}}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first two equations are the standard Maurer--Cartan structure on
the gauge Lie algebra $\Omega^{0,0}(X) \otimes \fg$ with ghost~$c$;
nilpotency $\delta_{\mathrm{BRST}}^2 \cA = [\bar\partial c + [\cA,c],
c] + [\cA, -\tfrac12[c,c]] = 0$ uses $\bar\partial[c,c] = 2[\bar\partial
c, c]$ and the Jacobi identity. The antifield transformations
in~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-brst-differential} are determined by requiring
the total differential $\delta_{\mathrm{tot}} = \delta_{\mathrm{KT}} +
\delta_{\mathrm{BRST}}$ to be nilpotent and to implement the Koszul--Tate
resolution with compatible BRST action. The compatibility
$\delta_{\mathrm{KT}} \delta_{\mathrm{BRST}} + \delta_{\mathrm{BRST}}
\delta_{\mathrm{KT}} = 0$ holds because
$\delta_{\mathrm{BRST}}(\delta_{\mathrm{KT}} \cA^*)
= \delta_{\mathrm{BRST}} F^{0,2}(\cA)
= \bar\partial(\bar\partial c + [\cA,c]) + [\cA, \bar\partial c + [\cA,c]]
= [F^{0,2}(\cA), c]
= \delta_{\mathrm{KT}}([\cA^*, c])$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{BV master action and classical master equation}
\label{subsec:6dhcs-bv-master-action}

\begin{theorem}[Classical BV master action solves the master equation;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{thm:6dhcs-bv-master-action}
\index{classical master equation!6d hCS}
The classical BV master action for 6d hCS is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-bv-master-action}
 S_{\mathrm{BV}}
 \;=\;
 S_0[\cA]
 + \int_{X^6} \Omega_X \wedge \langle \cA^*, \bar\partial c + [\cA, c]
 \rangle
 - \tfrac12 \int_{X^6} \Omega_X \wedge \langle c^*, [c, c]\rangle.
\end{equation}
It satisfies the classical BV master equation
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-cme}
 \{S_{\mathrm{BV}}, S_{\mathrm{BV}}\} \;=\; 0,
\end{equation}
and reduces to $S_0$ on the zero-antifield locus.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The antibracket $\{S_{\mathrm{BV}}, S_{\mathrm{BV}}\}$ decomposes as
classical ($0$-antifield) plus gauge-closure ($\cA^*$-linear) plus
Jacobi ($c^*$-linear) contributions. The $0$-antifield piece is the
gauge invariance of $S_0$, Lemma~\ref{lem:6dhcs-gauge}. The
$\cA^*$-linear piece is
\[
 2 \int \Omega_X \wedge \Bigl\langle \frac{\delta S_0}{\delta \cA},
 \bar\partial c + [\cA, c]\Bigr\rangle
 + \text{(quadratic in antifields)},
\]
which vanishes by the same gauge invariance evaluated on the EOM
$F^{0,2}(\cA) = \delta S_0 / \delta \cA$. The $c^*$-linear piece is
the Jacobi identity for $[c, c, c]$. Reduction to $S_0$ at zero
antifields is immediate.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The one-loop BV anomaly: explicit derivation, cancellation locus, Deligne series}
\label{subsec:6dhcs-qme}

The classical master equation $\{S_{\mathrm{BV}},S_{\mathrm{BV}}\}=0$
is the shell of gauge invariance; the quantum master equation
$\tfrac12\{S,S\}+\hbar\Delta_{\mathrm{BV}}S=0$ imposes that the
integration measure on the BV phase space be invariant under the
BRST flow. The coincident-point singularity of $\Delta_{\mathrm{BV}}$
forces a heat-kernel regularisation; the obstruction to extending
$S_{\mathrm{BV}}$ to a scale-$L$ solution of the quantum master
equation is the one-loop BV anomaly. What follows computes that
obstruction first-principles on $Y=\R^3\times K3\times\C^2$, pins
down both its Lie-algebra content and its field content, locates its
cancellation locus, and matches the answer simultaneously to
Costello--Li~\cite{CL16} and to the
Costello~\cite{Costello2017M5} M5-brane quartic-Casimir derivation,
consolidated by Costello--Francis--Gwilliam~\cite{CFG2026}.

\subsubsection{Heat-kernel regularisation and the BV Laplacian}
\label{subsubsec:6dhcs-heat-kernel}

The BV Laplacian
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-bv-laplacian}
 \Delta_{\mathrm{BV}}
 \;=\;
 \int_{Y} \Omega_Y \wedge
 \biggl(
 \frac{\delta^2}{\delta \cA \, \delta \cA^*}
 + \frac{\delta^2}{\delta c \, \delta c^*}
 \biggr)
\end{equation}
acts on local functionals by contracting a field with its antifield;
its kernel is the diagonal distribution $\delta_\Delta\in
\Omega^{0,0}(Y\times Y,\fg\otimes\fg)$, which is not a smooth form
and so $\Delta_{\mathrm{BV}}$ of a generic local functional is
ill-defined at coincident points. Costello's renormalisation
framework~\cite{costello-renormalization} replaces $\delta_\Delta$
by the scale-$L$ heat kernel
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-heat-kernel}
 K_t(x,y)
 \;=\;
 \sum_\lambda e^{-t\lambda}\,\phi_\lambda(x)\otimes\phi_\lambda(y)^*,
 \qquad
 (\bar\partial^*\bar\partial)\phi_\lambda \;=\; \lambda\,\phi_\lambda,
\end{equation}
where $\phi_\lambda$ is a complete eigenbasis of the Dolbeault
Laplacian $\square_{\bar\partial}=\bar\partial\bar\partial^*
+\bar\partial^*\bar\partial$ on
$\Omega^{0,\bullet}(Y)\otimes\fg$, and introduces the parametrix
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-parametrix}
 P_L \;=\; \int_0^L K_t\,dt,
 \qquad
 \bar\partial P_L \;=\; \mathrm{id} - K_L,
\end{equation}
so that $P_L$ inverts $\bar\partial$ up to a smoothing error $K_L$
that vanishes as $L\to 0$ and is smooth for $L>0$. The regularised
BV Laplacian $\Delta_L$ is $\Delta_{\mathrm{BV}}$ with $\delta_\Delta$
replaced by $K_L$, and the regularised quantum master equation at
scale $L$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-qme}
 \tfrac12 \{S^L_{\mathrm{BV}}, S^L_{\mathrm{BV}}\}
 + \hbar \Delta_L\,S^L_{\mathrm{BV}}
 \;=\; 0.
\end{equation}

\subsubsection{Renormalisation group flow and locality}
\label{subsubsec:6dhcs-rg-flow}

The scale-$L_1$ effective action $S^{L_1}$ determines the
scale-$L_2$ effective action $S^{L_2}$ through the Costello RG
equation
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-rg}
 S^{L_2}
 \;=\;
 \log\!\Bigl(\exp\bigl(\hbar\,\partial_{P_{L_1}^{L_2}}\bigr)
 \exp(S^{L_1}/\hbar)\Bigr),
 \qquad
 P_{L_1}^{L_2} \;=\; P_{L_2}-P_{L_1},
\end{equation}
which is the Feynman-graph expansion with edges replaced by the
connecting propagator $P_{L_1}^{L_2}$ and vertices drawn from
$S^{L_1}$. Locality of $S^L$ follows from the short-time asymptotic
$K_t(x,y)\sim t^{-3}e^{-|x-y|^2/(4t)}$ on $\dim_\bR Y=6$: each graph
integral decays as $e^{-L/\ell^2}$ in the coincidence limit, so
$S^L$ is a local functional up to exponentially small corrections.

\begin{definition}[Scale-$L$ effective action and obstruction cocycle;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{def:6dhcs-bv-laplacian}
\index{BV Laplacian!6d hCS}
\index{effective action!scale-$L$ hCS}
The scale-$L$ effective action $S^L_{\mathrm{BV}} = S_{\mathrm{BV}}
+\hbar S^{L,(1)}+O(\hbar^2)$ is the unique tree-exact lift of the
classical master action~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bv-master-action} that
satisfies~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-rg} and reduces to $S_{\mathrm{BV}}$ at
$L\to 0$. The one-loop obstruction cocycle is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-obs1-def}
 \mathrm{Obs}_1
 \;=\;
 \bigl(\hbar\Delta_L S^L_{\mathrm{BV}}\bigr)\big|_{\hbar^1}
 \;\bmod\; \{S_{\mathrm{BV}},-\}\,,
\end{equation}
an element of $H^1_{\mathrm{BRST}}(\Omega^{3,\bullet}(Y)
\otimes\Lambda^\bullet\fg^\vee)$ of ghost number $+1$.
\end{definition}

\subsubsection{One-loop tadpole and bubble graphs}
\label{subsubsec:6dhcs-tadpole}

The cubic interaction $\int\Omega_Y\wedge\tfrac16\langle\cA,
[\cA,\cA]\rangle$ and the ghost--field coupling
$\int\Omega_Y\wedge\langle\cA^*,[\cA,c]\rangle
-\tfrac12\int\Omega_Y\wedge\langle c^*,[c,c]\rangle$ contribute two
one-loop graphs to $\Delta_L S^L_{\mathrm{BV}}$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item the \emph{bubble graph} with two external $\cA$-legs and an
 internal $\cA\cA^*$-loop, contracted through
 $[\cA,c]\leftrightarrow[\cA^*,c]$ at the two vertices;
\item the \emph{box graph} with four external $c$-legs and an
 internal loop through the $\cA$-$\cA^*$-propagator at each of four
 vertices.
\end{enumerate}
The bubble graph~(i) produces the cubic-in-$\cA$ contribution of
Costello--Li~\cite{CL16}; the box graph~(ii) produces
the quartic-in-$c$ contribution of Costello~\cite{Costello2017M5}.
Both live in the same $H^1_{\mathrm{BRST}}$ cohomology class under
the descent equation, and the sum is the one-loop BV anomaly.

\begin{proposition}[Bubble contribution: cubic-$\cA$ cocycle;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{prop:6dhcs-bubble-cocycle}
\index{bubble graph!6d hCS}
The bubble graph contributes to~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-obs1-def} the
functional
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-bubble}
 \mathrm{Obs}_1^{\mathrm{bubble}}
 \;=\;
 \frac{h^\vee}{12}\,
 \int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge
 \mathrm{tr}_{\mathrm{fund}}\bigl(\cA\wedge\bar\partial\cA
 \wedge\bar\partial c\bigr).
\end{equation}
On the $\cA^*$-free sector, the equivalent cubic-$\cA$ cocycle
obtained by descent is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-bubble-descent}
 \mathrm{Obs}_1^{\mathrm{bubble,desc}}
 \;=\;
 \frac{c_2(\fg)}{24}
 \int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge
 \mathrm{tr}_{\mathrm{fund}}\bigl(\cA\wedge\bar\partial\cA
 \wedge\bar\partial\cA\bigr),
 \qquad
 c_2(\fg)\;=\;\frac{\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(T^aT_a)}{\dim\fg}
 \;=\; 2h^\vee\ \ (\fg\text{ simple}).
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The bubble graph is the contraction of the BV Laplacian with the
cubic vertex $\int\cA^*[\cA,c]$, producing
\[
 \Delta_L\!\left(\int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge\langle\cA^*,[\cA,c]\rangle\right)
 \;=\;
 \int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge
 \sum_{a,b}[T^a,T^b]^c\,
 \bigl(P_L\cdot\mathrm{ad}_c\bigr)_{ab}\,
 \bar\partial\cA^c,
\]
with $P_L$ the regularised $\bar\partial$-propagator evaluated at
the diagonal and $\mathrm{ad}_c$ the adjoint action of the ghost.
Taking the ad-trace yields the structure-constant contraction
$f^{acd}f^{bcd}=2h^\vee\delta^{ab}$; the combinatorial factor $1/24$
is the Bernoulli prefactor $|B_2|/(2\cdot 2!)=1/24$ from the scale-$L$
heat-kernel integration
$\int_0^L t^{-1}\mathrm{vol}(Y)\,dt=L^{-1}\cdot\tfrac{1}{24}
+\text{counterterm}$ on a Calabi--Yau 3-fold~\cite[Ch.\,5
Thm.\,5.4]{costello-renormalization}; this is the same
$|B_2|/4 = 1/24$ that fixes the genus-$1$ Hodge integral
$\int_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{1,1}}\lambda_1$. Descending to the
$\cA$-sector via $\bar\partial c\mapsto\cA$ (the Faddeev--Popov
identification $c\leftrightarrow\text{gauge parameter}$) converts
$\bar\partial c$ to $\cA$ inside the $\bar\partial$-exact
representative of~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bubble} and yields the cubic-$\cA$
cocycle~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bubble-descent}. The coefficient
$c_2(\fg)/24$ matches the
Costello--Li~\cite[eq.\,(6.12)]{CL16} anomaly for
holomorphic Chern--Simons on a CY 3-fold.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Box contribution: quartic-$c$ cocycle;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{prop:6dhcs-box-cocycle}
\index{box graph!6d hCS}
The box graph contributes to~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-obs1-def} the
functional
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-box}
 \mathrm{Obs}_1^{\mathrm{box}}
 \;=\;
 \frac{1}{(2\pi i)^3}\,\frac{1}{4!}\,
 \mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}
 \bigl(T^aT^bT^cT^d\bigr)_{\mathrm{sym}}
 \int_Y \Omega_Y\wedge
 c^a\wedge\bar\partial c^b\wedge\bar\partial c^c\wedge\bar\partial c^d,
\end{equation}
where $(T^aT^bT^cT^d)_{\mathrm{sym}}$ denotes symmetrisation over
$(a,b,c,d)$. The integrand is a $(3,3)$-top-form on $Y$: $\Omega_Y$
provides holomorphic degree $(3,0)$, $c$ contributes $(0,0)$, and
the three $\bar\partial c$'s contribute $(0,3)$; the combination
$(3,0)\wedge(0,0)\wedge(0,3)=(3,3)$ saturates the holomorphic and
antiholomorphic top degrees.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The box graph has four cubic vertices $\int\cA^*[\cA,c]$ and four
$\bar\partial$-propagators closing the loop in the
$\cA\cA^*$-direction. The four external legs are therefore $c$'s.
Each vertex contributes one structure-constant factor and one
$\bar\partial$ (the derivative on the ghost $c$), giving four
factors $f^{abe_1}f^{bce_2}f^{cde_3}f^{dae_4}$ summed over the
internal indices $e_1,e_2,e_3,e_4$, which assembles to the
adjoint symmetric quartic trace
$\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(T^aT^bT^cT^d)_{\mathrm{sym}}$. The
combinatorial factor $1/4!$ is the symmetry factor of the box. The
overall $(2\pi i)^{-3}$ is the Cauchy kernel normalisation of the
three $\bar\partial$-propagators entering the loop; on a CY 3-fold
each $\bar\partial^{-1}$ carries $(2\pi i)^{-1}$. The result is the
six-dimensional Chern-character anomaly
$\mathrm{ch}_4(F)=(2\pi i)^{-4}\mathrm{Tr}F^4/4!$ integrated against
$\Omega_Y$, reduced to the ghost sector by the descent
$F=\bar\partial c+\tfrac12[c,c]\mapsto\bar\partial c$ at ghost
degree one. This is Costello--Francis--Gwilliam~\cite{CFG2026}
specialised to 6d hCS.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[One-loop BV anomaly of 6d hCS: canonical form;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{thm:6dhcs-one-loop-anomaly}
\index{anomaly!one-loop 6d hCS}
\index{quantum master equation!6d hCS obstruction}
The one-loop BV anomaly~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-obs1-def} of 6d holomorphic
Chern--Simons on a Calabi--Yau 3-fold $Y$ with gauge algebra $\fg$
is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-anomaly-cocycle}
 \mathrm{Obs}_1
 \;=\;
 \frac{c_2(\fg)}{24}
 \int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge
 \mathrm{tr}_{\mathrm{fund}}\bigl(\cA\wedge\bar\partial\cA
 \wedge\bar\partial\cA\bigr)
 \;+\;
 \frac{1}{(2\pi i)^3\cdot 4!}\,
 \mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}
 \bigl(T^aT^bT^cT^d\bigr)_{\mathrm{sym}}
 \int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge
 c^a\,\bar\partial c^b\,\bar\partial c^c\,\bar\partial c^d.
\end{equation}
The two summands~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bubble-descent}
and~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-box} are $\bar\partial$-closed representatives
of the same $H^1_{\mathrm{BRST}}$ cohomology class under the descent
equation $F=\bar\partial c+\tfrac12[c,c]$; the cubic-$\cA$ form is
the descent image of the quartic-$c$ form, and conversely the
quartic-$c$ form is the ghost-lift of the cubic-$\cA$ form. Both
forms are simultaneously present in the scale-$L$ effective action
and combine, via~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-obs1-def}, to the canonical BV
anomaly class
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-canonical-BV-anomaly}
 [\mathrm{Obs}_1]
 \;=\;
 \frac{1}{(2\pi i)^3}\,
 \int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge
 \mathrm{ch}_4^{\mathrm{ad}}(\cA),
\end{equation}
the integral of the adjoint fourth Chern character against the
holomorphic volume form.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The sum of the two graph contributions is
Propositions~\ref{prop:6dhcs-bubble-cocycle}
and~\ref{prop:6dhcs-box-cocycle}. The descent identification is
the six-dimensional version of
Costello~\cite[Prop.\,3.4]{Costello2017M5}: the 4-form
$\mathrm{ch}_4(F)$ evaluated on the pure BRST curvature
$F=\bar\partial c+\tfrac12[c,c]$ decomposes under ghost grading as
$\mathrm{ch}_4(\bar\partial c+\tfrac12[c,c])
=\mathrm{ch}_4(\bar\partial c)+\text{ghost-expansion corrections}$,
and the ghost-degree-4 piece $\tfrac{1}{4!}\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}
(\bar\partial c)^4$ is the box graph~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-box}, while
the ghost-degree-0 piece
$\tfrac{1}{4!}\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}F^4|_{c=0}
=\tfrac{1}{4!}\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(\bar\partial\cA
+\tfrac12[\cA,\cA])^2$ is, after integration by parts and use of
$\bar\partial\Omega_Y=0$, the bubble
graph~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bubble-descent} with coefficient
$c_2(\fg)/24$. The relative normalisation $c_2(\fg)/24$ versus
$1/(4!\cdot(2\pi i)^3)$ is the Hodge--Hirzebruch factor on the
6-real-dimensional $Y$: the fourth Chern character
$\mathrm{ch}_4=\tfrac{1}{4!}\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}F^4$ integrated
against $\Omega_Y\in\Omega^{3,0}$ reduces, at cubic order in $\cA$,
to the bubble coefficient $c_2(\fg)/24$ through
$\mathrm{ch}_2^{\mathrm{ad}}=c_2(\fg)/2$ and the Bernoulli
$1/12\cdot 1/2=1/24$ heat-kernel integration on CY 3-folds. Both
coefficients are shadows of the single adjoint $\mathrm{ch}_4$
class~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-canonical-BV-anomaly}.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Cancellation locus and the Deligne exceptional series}
\label{subsubsec:6dhcs-cancellation-locus}

\begin{corollary}[Cancellation locus of the 6d hCS BV anomaly;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{cor:6dhcs-deligne-cancellation}
\index{anomaly cancellation!6d hCS}
\index{Deligne exceptional series!anomaly cancellation}
The anomaly~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-anomaly-cocycle} vanishes on the
following loci in the space of simple Lie algebras.  The
gauge-theoretic cancellation condition is the quartic trace identity
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-quartic-trace-identity}
 \Tr_{\fg}(X^4)
 =
 \lambda_{\fg}\bigl(\Tr_{\fg}(X^2)\bigr)^2
 \qquad(X\in\fg),
\end{equation}
possibly after the listed Green--Schwarz/axion counterterm data are
supplied:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item \emph{Abelian locus.} $\fg=\fu(1)^{\oplus r}$, for which
 $c_2(\fg)=0$, $h^\vee=0$, and
 $\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(T^aT^bT^cT^d)\equiv 0$ because the
 adjoint representation is trivial. All terms of
 \eqref{eq:6dhcs-anomaly-cocycle} vanish identically.
\item \emph{Critical Kac--Moody twist.} The bubble
 piece~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bubble-descent} is absorbed by a $K^{-1/2}$
 counterterm twisting the level to $k=-h^\vee$~\cite{Costello2013};
 on the chiral-avatar curve this is the Feigin--Frenkel critical
 level, and the 6d hCS boundary algebra is
 $\widehat\fg_{-h^\vee}\otimes K^{-1/2}$.
\item \emph{Deligne exceptional series minus $E_6$ and
 $A_2$-unrefined.} On
 $\mathrm{Deligne}^{\mathrm{exc}}\setminus\{E_6,\,A_2\text{-unrefined}\}
 =\{A_1,G_2,D_4,F_4,E_7,E_8\}\cup\{A_2\text{-refined}\}$ the
 adjoint symmetric quartic trace factors through the square of
 the quadratic Casimir,
 \begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-deligne-factor}
 \mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}\bigl(T^aT^bT^cT^d\bigr)_{\mathrm{sym}}
 \;=\;
 \alpha_\fg\cdot\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(T^{(a}T^b)
 \mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(T^cT^{d)})\bigr),
 \end{equation}
 with $\alpha_\fg$ the Vogel parameter~\cite{Deligne1996};
 the factored piece is BV-exact against the cubic
 $\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(T^aT^b)\int c^a\wedge\bar\partial c^b$
 counterterm. The six algebras $\{A_1,G_2,D_4,F_4,E_7,E_8\}$
 sit unconditionally inside this locus: the quartic factorisation
 combines with vanishing cubic adjoint trace
 $d^{abc}=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(T^{(a}T^bT^{c)})\equiv 0$ to
 kill both obstructions. $A_2=\mathfrak{su}(3)$ carries a nonzero
 Gell-Mann cubic $d^{abc}$ and enters the locus only in its
 refined form: the Feigin--Frenkel critical-level $K^{-1/2}$ twist
 of clause~(b) kills the quadratic obstruction, and the
 Dimofte-slab anomaly-inflow of Part~V provides the
 Green--Schwarz cubic cancellation of $d^{abc}\int c^a\wedge
 c^b\wedge\bar\partial c^c$; the combined refinement places
 $A_2$-refined inside the cancellation locus, while $A_2$-unrefined
 remains outside.
\item \emph{$E_6$ strict exclusion.} $E_6$ carries the Jordan
 cubic invariant $d^{abc}$ on $\mathrm{Sym}^3(\mathbf{27})=
 \mathfrak j_3^{\mathbb O}$, so
 $\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(T^{(a}T^bT^{c)})\neq 0$ on $E_6$
 and~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-deligne-factor} alone does not kill the
 anomaly. The $K^{-1/2}$ critical twist addresses the quadratic
 obstruction, not the cubic; no refinement in the BV toolkit
 produces a cubic Green--Schwarz counterterm compatible with the
 Jordan structure on $\mathbf{27}$. $E_6$ is therefore strictly
 excluded from the cancellation locus of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:6dhcs-one-loop-anomaly}.
\item \emph{Super-algebras with vanishing supertrace.} For
 $\fg=\mathfrak{gl}(n|n)$ or any super Lie algebra with
 $\mathrm{str}_{\mathrm{ad}}\equiv 0$, both
 $c_2^{\mathrm{str}}(\fg)$ and the supersymmetric quartic Casimir
 vanish; the 6d super-hCS is anomaly-free. This is the
 Costello--Francis--Gwilliam~\cite{CFG2026} observation relevant
 for holomorphic Chern--Simons coupled to twisted supergravity.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
\textup{(a)} Direct: abelian structure constants vanish.
\textup{(b)} Costello~\cite[\S4]{Costello2013}: the $K^{-1/2}$
counterterm adds a BV-exact term to $S_{\mathrm{BV}}$ proportional
to
$\int\Omega_Y\wedge\langle\cA,\partial\log\det(K_Y^{1/2})\wedge\cA
\rangle$; its $\Delta_L$-variation cancels the bubble
piece~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bubble-descent} with coefficient
$c_2(\fg)/24$ at the critical-level twist $k=-h^\vee$.
\textup{(c)} Deligne~\cite{Deligne1996} characterises the series
by the existence of a universal formula for $\dim(V_\lambda)$ with
$\lambda$ the Young-diagram label, in terms of the Vogel parameter
$t=\alpha_\fg/h^\vee$; the quartic-Casimir
factorisation~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-deligne-factor} is a consequence of
the $V_\lambda^{\otimes 2}$ decomposition being a sum of Young
diagrams whose characters are polynomial in the quadratic Casimir.
On $\{A_1,G_2,D_4,F_4,E_7,E_8\}$ the cubic adjoint trace
$d^{abc}=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(T^{(a}T^bT^{c)})$ vanishes
identically (representation-theoretic fact; Cvitanović 2008
Ch.~20), so the factored quartic is BV-exact against the cubic
counterterm and $\mathrm{Obs}_1^{\mathrm{box}}=0$ up to
$\{S_{\mathrm{BV}},-\}$. For $A_2=\mathfrak{su}(3)$, the live
Gell-Mann $d^{abc}$ requires the Dimofte-slab anomaly-inflow
of Part~V: the slab provides a three-form counterterm
$\omega_{\mathrm{slab}}\in H^3_{\mathrm{BRST}}(\partial Y)$ whose
BV-variation absorbs $d^{abc}\int c^a\wedge c^b\wedge\bar\partial
c^c$ via Green--Schwarz descent, placing $A_2$-refined (critical
twist of clause~(b) composed with slab inflow) inside the
locus; $A_2$-unrefined sits outside.
\textup{(d)} The supertrace $\mathrm{str}$ on $\mathfrak{gl}(n|n)$
satisfies $\mathrm{str}(\mathrm{id})=n-n=0$, and the Killing form is
degenerate; the adjoint quartic supertrace factors as $n$-even
$-$ $n$-odd $=0$ by supersymmetry.
\textup{(e)} $E_6$ strict exclusion. The Jordan cubic
$d^{abc}=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(T^{(a}T^bT^{c)})$ on
$\mathbf{27}=\mathfrak j_3^{\mathbb O}$ is nonzero
(Cvitanović~2008~Ch.~20; Baez~2002); the quartic factorisation
of~(c) kills
$\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}(T^{(a}T^bT^cT^{d)})_{\mathrm{sym}}$ only
modulo the cubic-counterterm BV variation, which is not BV-exact on
$E_6$ because no choice of $K^{-1/2}$-twist or slab-inflow in the
programme's toolkit trivialises the Jordan cubic at the
BV-cohomology level. $E_6$ therefore retains a nontrivial anomaly
class in $H^1_{\mathrm{BRST}}(\Omega^{3,\bullet}(Y))$ and sits
outside the cancellation locus.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[$\mathrm{Sp}(2N)$ and $\mathrm{SO}(N)$]
\label{rem:6dhcs-sp-so}
\index{symplectic series!anomaly cancellation}
\index{orthogonal series!anomaly cancellation}
The Deligne series contains $A_1=\mathrm{SU}(2)\cong\mathrm{Sp}(2)$
as the smallest symplectic entry and $D_4=\mathrm{SO}(8)$ as the
smallest orthogonal entry with triality. Higher
$\mathrm{Sp}(2N\geq 4)$ and $\mathrm{SO}(N\neq 8)$ are \emph{not}
in the Deligne series: for $\mathrm{Sp}(2N)$ with $N\geq 2$, the
adjoint quartic Casimir has an independent component orthogonal to
$(\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{ad}}T^2)^2$,
so~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-deligne-factor} fails, and the 6d hCS BV
anomaly does not vanish. Combined with the strict exclusion of
$E_6$ via its Jordan cubic and the split of $A_2$ into refined
(inside) and unrefined (outside) sectors, the cancellation locus
for the 6d hCS one-loop BV anomaly on a CY$_3$ reads
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-canonical-anom-locus}
\mathrm{Anom}_1=0\;\iff\;\fg\in
\bigl(\mathrm{Deligne}^{\mathrm{exc}}\setminus
\{E_6,\,A_2\text{-unrefined}\}\bigr)
\cup\{\mathrm{abelian}\}
\cup\{\mathrm{super\text{-}str}_{\mathrm{ad}}=0\}
\cup\{\widehat\fg_{-h^\vee}\otimes K^{-1/2}\text{-refined}\}.
\end{equation}
$A_2$-refined carries the Feigin--Frenkel critical-level twist
together with the Dimofte-slab anomaly-inflow from Part~V.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Why two coefficients, one anomaly: the Chern--Simons
 descent]
\label{rem:6dhcs-descent}
\index{Chern--Simons descent!6d hCS}
The $c_2(\fg)/24$ coefficient is the bubble piece (cubic-$\cA$
field content); the adjoint quartic Casimir is the box piece
(quartic-$c$ field content); the two are related by the
Chern--Simons descent $\mathrm{ch}_4(F)\to\mathrm{CS}_7(\cA)\to
\mathrm{Obs}_1$ at ghost degrees $0$ and $4$ respectively. The
unified statement is that $[\mathrm{Obs}_1]$ is the integral of
$\mathrm{ch}_4^{\mathrm{ad}}$ against $\Omega_Y$, and the
Deligne-series cancellation is the factorisation of
$\mathrm{ch}_4^{\mathrm{ad}}$ through
$\mathrm{ch}_2^{\mathrm{ad}}\wedge\mathrm{ch}_2^{\mathrm{ad}}$,
which is BV-exact against the $K^{-1/2}$-counterterm. The bubble
and box pieces are cohomologous in $H^1_{\mathrm{BRST}}$; choosing
one or the other is a choice of representative, not of anomaly.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{The $\chi(K3)=24$ factor on $Y=\R^3\times K3\times\C^2$}
\label{subsubsec:6dhcs-chi-K3}

\begin{proposition}[The \(K3\) Euler factor and Jacobi-form convention;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{prop:6dhcs-chi-K3-24}
\index{Euler characteristic!$K3$, $=24$}
\index{$24$-factor!$\zeta(2)$ and $\chi(K3)$}
On $Y=\R^3\times K3\times\C^2$, the bubble coefficient $c_2(\fg)/24$
of~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bubble-descent} receives a geometric factor
$\chi(K3)=24$ upon integration over the $K3$ factor:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-K3-integration}
 \frac{1}{24}\int_{K3} e(T_{K3})
 \;=\;
 \frac{\chi(K3)}{24}
 \;=\; 1.
\end{equation}
The cancellation is the product of two independent numbers: the
heat-kernel Bernoulli denominator \(1/24=|B_2|/4=\zeta(2)/(2\pi)^2\)
and the Euler number \(\chi(K3)=24\). The Jacobi-form convention is separate:
\[
 Z_{\mathrm{ell}}(K3;\tau,z)=2\phi_{0,1}^{K3}(\tau,z),\qquad
 \phi_{0,1}^{K3}=y^{-1}+10+y+O(q).
\]
The full K3 elliptic genus supplies the Euler number by
\(Z_{\mathrm{ell}}(K3;\tau,0)=24\), while the half-genus supplies the
BKM weight by
\(c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(0)/2=10/2=5\).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{One-loop Feynman}: $|B_2|/4=1/24$ is the
 $\zeta$-regularised coefficient $|\zeta(-1)|=1/12$ halved by the
 $\mathrm{Sp}(2,\bZ)$ doubling, and equals
 $\zeta(2)/(2\pi)^2=1/24$.
\item \emph{Topology}: $\chi(K3)=24$ from Euler-class integration
 $\int_{K3}e(T_{K3})=24$, equivalently $24$ nodal singular fibres
 of the generic elliptic $K3$.
\item \emph{Arithmetic / automorphic}: the half K3 weak Jacobi form
 \(\phi_{0,1}^{K3}\) has \(q^0\)-term \(y^{-1}+10+y\); the \(24\) is
 \(2\phi_{0,1}^{K3}(\tau,0)\), not the coefficient defining the
 Borcherds weight~\cite{EguchiOoguriTachikawa2011}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\textup{(i)} The heat-kernel trace on a CY 3-fold evaluates
$\int_0^\infty K_t(x,x)_{\mathrm{reg}}\,dt$ against the holomorphic
volume form; the leading regularised coefficient is $|B_2|/4=1/24$,
the Bernoulli number $B_2=1/6$ entering the
$\zeta(s)$-regularised sum $\zeta(-1)=-1/12$, paired with the
doubling from $\zeta(2)/(2\pi)^2=1/24$ on the Riemann-surface
boundary; the factor $1/24=\zeta(2)\cdot(2\pi)^{-2}$ is the
Casson--Gordon--Milgram $\zeta(2)$-denominator.
\textup{(ii)} The Euler characteristic of $K3$ is $\chi(K3)=24$ by
Euler-class integration:
\[
 \chi(K3)=\int_{K3}e(T_{K3})
 =\int_{K3}c_2(T_{K3})=24 .
\]
Equivalently, a generic elliptic \(K3\) fibration has \(24\) nodal
singular fibres. The Hirzebruch signature formula is a separate check:
\(\sigma(K3)=-16=\frac13\int_{K3}p_1(T_{K3})\), with
\(p_1=c_1^2-2c_2=-2c_2\) because \(c_1(T_{K3})=0\).
\textup{(iii)} The $K3$ elliptic genus is
\(Z_{\mathrm{ell}}(K3;\tau,z)=2\phi_{0,1}^{K3}(\tau,z)\), hence
\(Z_{\mathrm{ell}}(K3;\tau,0)=24\). The coefficient
\(c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(0)=10\) is the Jacobi coefficient governing the
\(\Delta_5\) Borcherds weight.

The one-loop Feynman trace and the Euler characteristic multiply to the
unit in \eqref{eq:6dhcs-K3-integration}. Hirzebruch--Riemann--Roch on
\(K3\) is the compatible index check
\[
\chi(K3,\mathcal{O})=\int_{K3}\operatorname{td}(T_{K3})=2,\qquad
\chi(K3,T_{K3})=\int_{K3}\operatorname{ch}(T_{K3})\operatorname{td}(T_{K3})=-20.
\]
It does not produce the heat-kernel denominator. The bubble cancellation
uses two independent inputs: the analytic Bernoulli coefficient from
\textup{(i)} and the Euler integral from \textup{(ii)}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-24-unified}
 \left(\frac{|B_2|}{4}\right)\int_{K3}e(T_{K3})
 =
 \frac{1}{24}\cdot 24
 =
 1 .
\end{equation}
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Borcherds product for the anomaly characteristic series}
\label{subsubsec:6dhcs-borcherds-exp}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional Borcherds characteristic target of the BV
anomaly tower; \ClaimStatusConditional]
\label{thm:6dhcs-borcherds-exp}
\index{Borcherds product!BV anomaly exponentiation}
\index{$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}$!BV anomaly characteristic series}
The abelian 6d hCS anomaly characteristic series on
$X=\R^3\times K3\times T^2$ at abelian gauge algebra $\fg=\fgl_1$
(where the gauge-theoretic one-loop anomaly cancels by
Corollary~\ref{cor:6dhcs-deligne-cancellation}\textup{(a)}) has, after choosing
a scalar Heegner characteristic map satisfying the source BV/hCS,
chain-map, parity/supertrace, root-label, denominator-normalisation,
and counterterm hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bvbrst-heegner-all-order-vol2}, the Borcherds target
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-borcherds-product}
 C_{K3}^{\mathrm{ab}}(Z;\hbar)
 \;:=\;
 \sum_{g\geq 1}\hbar^g\,\chi_{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathrm{Obs}_g),
 \qquad
 \chi_{\mathrm{ab}}\bigl(\mathcal Z_{\hCS}^{\mathrm{anom}}(X,\hbar)\bigr)
 \;=\;
 \Bigl(\frac{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z)}
 {\eta(\tau)^{24}\eta(\tau')^{24}}\Bigr)^{C_{K3}^{\mathrm{ab}}(Z;\hbar)},
\end{equation}
with $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2$ the Gritsenko--Nikulin
weight-$10$ Igusa square in the non-OP convention
~\cite{GritsenkoNikulin1998,DijkgraafVerlindeVerlinde1997},
\(\eta\) the Dedekind eta function, and exponent
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-cK3}
 C_{K3}^{\mathrm{ab}}(Z;\hbar)
 \;=\; \sum_{\substack{g\geq1\\ g\equiv0,3\pmod 4}}
 \hbar^g c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(a_g,r_g)\cdot H_g
\end{equation}
encoding the scalar Heegner projection of the $g$-loop obstruction at
each genus, where
\(4a_g-r_g^2=g\) is the chosen positive-cone root label for the
K3 Borcherds input, and
\(H_g\) is the \(g\)-th Humbert class in
$\mathrm{CH}^1(\mathcal{A}_2)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The source abelian anomaly has the ordinary one-loop cancellation of
Corollary~\ref{cor:6dhcs-deligne-cancellation}\textup{(a)}. The passage from its
loop obstruction tower to Heegner classes is the assumed scalar
characteristic map \(\chi_{\mathrm{ab}}\). After applying that map,
Proposition~\ref{prop:6dhcs-chi-K3-24} fixes the \(K3\) Euler
normalisation and the Heegner coefficient is a Fourier coefficient of
\(\phi_{0,1}^{K3}\). The characteristic exponentiation
\[
 \chi_{\mathrm{ab}}\bigl(\mathcal Z_{\hCS}^{\mathrm{anom}}(X,\hbar)\bigr)
 \;=\;
 \exp\!\Bigl(\sum_{g\geq 1}\hbar^g\,\chi_{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathrm{Obs}_g)\,
 \log\frac{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z)}
 {\eta(\tau)^{24}\eta(\tau')^{24}}\Bigr)
\]
is the Borcherds product formula
\[
\log\Delta_5
=\log\bigl(64(qyq')^{1/2}\bigr)
+\sum_{n,r,m>0}c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(nm,r)
\log(1-q^ny^rq'^m),
\qquad
\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2
\]
~\cite[Thm.\,4.1]{GritsenkoNikulin1998}. The
identification of the exponent with \(C_{K3}^{\mathrm{ab}}(Z;\hbar)\) is the
Dijkgraaf--Verlinde--Verlinde~\cite[eq.\,(5.22)]{DijkgraafVerlindeVerlinde1997}
second-quantised string identity on $K3$; the two eta factors are the
separating-boundary elliptic discriminant factors. This proves the
scalar automorphic target, not an
unconditional equality of the abelian hCS anomaly series with a
Borcherds product before applying \(\chi_{\mathrm{ab}}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The \(24\)-factor and the Jacobi coefficient]
\label{rem:6dhcs-three-24}
\index{$24$-factor!Euler characteristic}
The \(24\) in~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-borcherds-product} is the exponent of
each separating-boundary \(\eta^{-24}\) factor and comes from
\(\chi(K3)\). The same \(24\) cancels the
heat-kernel denominator \(1/24\) in
Proposition~\ref{prop:6dhcs-chi-K3-24}. It is not the coefficient
defining the Borcherds weight. The BKM weight uses the half-genus
coefficient \(c_{\phi_{0,1}^{K3}}(0)/2=10/2=5\), while the full
specialisation \(2\phi_{0,1}^{K3}(\tau,0)=24\) records the Euler
characteristic.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Relation to Costello 2017 M5 and Costello--Li 2020;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{rem:6dhcs-CFG-CL-costello17}
\index{Costello 2017 M5!6d hCS anomaly}
\index{Costello--Li 2020!6d hCS anomaly}
\index{Costello--Francis--Gwilliam 2026!6d hCS anomaly}
The box piece~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-box} with the adjoint quartic
Casimir matches~\cite[eq.\,(5.17)]{Costello2017M5} after evaluation
on the $\R^3$-topological slab of $X=\R^3\times K3\times\C^2$; the
bubble piece~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bubble-descent} with coefficient
$c_2(\fg)/24$ matches~\cite[eq.\,(6.12)]{CL16} on the
holomorphic $\C^3$ slab; both are cohomologous in
$H^1_{\mathrm{BRST}}$ by
Theorem~\ref{thm:6dhcs-one-loop-anomaly}. The
Costello--Francis--Gwilliam~\cite{CFG2026} refinement shows that
the full $\mathrm{ch}_4^{\mathrm{ad}}$ anomaly is the only
one-loop gauge anomaly in this sector; higher-loop terms are organised
by the Humbert-class tower~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-cK3}. Absorption into a
strict quantum action is equivalent to vanishing of the counterterm and
QME obstruction classes of
Proposition~\ref{prop:costello-trace-counterterms-vanish} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:costello-trace-qme-solvable}.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Factorisation algebra of classical and quantum observables}
\label{subsec:6dhcs-facalg}

\begin{definition}[Classical and quantum observable
 factorisation algebras;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{def:6dhcs-obs}
\index{factorisation algebra!6d hCS observables}
The classical observable factorisation algebra
$\mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}}$ of 6d hCS assigns to each open
$U \subset X^6$ the cochain complex
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-cl-obs}
 \mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}}(U)
 \;=\;
 \bigl(
 \mathrm{Sym}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{BV}}(U)^\vee\bigr),\;
 \{S_{\mathrm{BV}}|_U, -\}
 \bigr),
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{BV}}(U)$ is the restriction to~$U$ of the
BV phase space~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bv-phase-space}. The quantum
observable factorisation algebra
$\mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{q}}$ is the Costello--Gwilliam $\bC[[\hbar]]$-
deformation of~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-cl-obs} by the BV Laplacian,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-q-obs}
 \mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{q}}(U)
 \;=\;
 \bigl(
 \mathrm{Sym}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{BV}}(U)^\vee\bigr)[[\hbar]],\;
 \{S_{\mathrm{BV}}|_U, -\} + \hbar \Delta_{\mathrm{BV}}
 \bigr).
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Classical-limit compatibility;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{thm:6dhcs-cl-limit}
\index{classical limit!6d hCS observables}
At $\hbar \to 0$,
\[
 \mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{q}} / \hbar \mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{q}}
 \;\cong\;
 \mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}}
\]
as factorisation algebras of cochain complexes on~$X^6$, and this
isomorphism intertwines the factorisation-product structure induced
by disjoint unions $U_1 \sqcup U_2 \subset U$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The Sym-coalgebra structure is independent of~$\hbar$; the
differential on~$\mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{q}}/\hbar$ is the $\hbar \to 0$
specialisation of~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-q-obs}, which drops the
$\hbar \Delta_{\mathrm{BV}}$ term and recovers~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-cl-obs}.
Factorisation-product compatibility is Costello--Gwilliam I.5.7.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Comparison with Henneaux--Teitelboim and
 Costello--one compact summary]
\label{rem:6dhcs-ht-costello}
\index{Henneaux--Teitelboim!6d hCS comparison}
The Koszul--Tate resolution of
Proposition~\ref{prop:6dhcs-kt-resolution} is the generalisation
of~\cite[\S10]{HenneauxTeitelboim1992} from finite-dimensional gauge
systems to the infinite-dimensional $\Omega^{0,\bullet}(X^6) \otimes
\fg$ complex, with regular-sequence hypothesis verified by the
transversality of holomorphic flatness. The BV master
action~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bv-master-action} is the first-order
reducible case of~\cite[\S18.2]{HenneauxTeitelboim1992}.
The renormalised BV Laplacian and the factorisation-algebra
formulation of $\mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{q}}$ are the Calabi--Yau
3-fold instance of~\cite[Ch.~5]{costello-renormalization}; the
quartic-Casimir anomaly cocycle~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-anomaly-cocycle} is
the six-dimensional version of the Costello perturbative anomaly
descent, with Deligne-series cancellation as in
\cite{Costello2017M5}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Koszul--Tate, chiral $r(z)$, and the shadow tower]
\label{rem:6dhcs-rmatrix-shadow}
\index{r-matrix!6d hCS Koszul--Tate origin}
The 6d hCS Koszul--Tate complex on $\bC^3$ localised to a formal
neighbourhood of $z = 0$ in the second $\bC$-direction produces the
chiral $r(z)$-matrix (seven-face presentation): the
first-order antifield sector $\cA^* \leftrightarrow \bar\partial c$
contributes the rational $k\,\Omega_{\mathrm{tr}}/z$ piece
(Vol~I, \texttt{chapters/examples/landscape\_census.tex}), the
second-order sector $c^* \leftrightarrow [c,c]$ contributes the
higher-pole corrections, and the $\Omega_X$-measure enforces the
Calabi--Yau trace condition that selects the level~$k$ normalisation.
The shadow tower $\{S_2, S_3, S_4, \ldots\}$ of Vol~I encodes the
residual quartic-Casimir class as an algebraic shadow. It does not
construct the gauge-theoretic Green--Schwarz/axion counterterm that
trivializes the one-loop BV anomaly; that cancellation is precisely the
extra datum isolated in
Corollary~\ref{cor:6dhcs-deligne-cancellation}. Thus the shadow tower
detects the quartic residue, while 6d hCS BV--BRST supplies the
cancellation mechanism only after the trace-factorisation and
counterterm hypotheses are present.
\end{remark}

\section{BV deformation theory of 6d holomorphic Chern--Simons}
\label{sec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-deformation}

\index{BV deformation theory!6d holomorphic Chern--Simons|textbf}
\index{L-infinity algebra!6d hCS gauge fields|textbf}
\index{Maurer--Cartan!6d hCS|textbf}
\index{Kodaira--Spencer!6d hCS avatar|textbf}

The classical master action, the Koszul--Tate resolution and the
one-loop anomaly of 6d holomorphic Chern--Simons are
recorded in~\S\ref{sec:vol2-bvbrst-kt-6dhcs}. The underlying
\emph{$L_\infty$ deformation theory} of Costello--Francis--Gwilliam
\cite{CFG2026} requires four data: the homotopy Lie
algebra controlling classical fields; the intersection of its
Maurer--Cartan locus with Kodaira--Spencer moduli; the
$(-1)$-shifted symplectic form on the shifted cotangent; and the
content of $H^1$ and $H^2$ of this $L_\infty$ algebra for
$Y = \R^3\times K3\times\C^2$. Each is constructed below in the
order in which Costello--Francis--Gwilliam present them for the
$E_3$ case, followed by the side-by-side dictionary.

\subsection{The $L_\infty$-algebra of gauge fields}
\label{subsec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-linf}

\begin{definition}[Gauge-field $L_\infty$-algebra;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{def:6dhcs-gauge-linf}
\index{L-infinity algebra!gauge-field of 6d hCS}
Let $Y$ be a complex three-fold with a nowhere-vanishing holomorphic
volume form $\Omega_Y\in\Omega^{3,0}(Y)$, and let $\fg$ be a
finite-dimensional complex Lie algebra with invariant pairing
$\langle-,-\rangle\colon\fg\otimes\fg\to\bC$. The \emph{gauge-field
$L_\infty$-algebra} of 6d holomorphic Chern--Simons is the
$\bZ$-graded vector space
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-gauge-linf-space}
 \cL_Y \;=\; \Omega^{0,\bullet}(Y)\otimes\fg,
 \qquad
 \cL_Y^m \;=\; \Omega^{0,m}(Y)\otimes\fg,
\end{equation}
shifted so that gauge-ghost degree $m$ corresponds to cohomological
degree $m$. The brackets are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-gauge-linf-brackets}
 \ell_1 \;=\; \bar\partial,
 \qquad
 \ell_2(\alpha_1,\alpha_2) \;=\; [\alpha_1,\alpha_2],
 \qquad
 \ell_n \;=\; 0 \text{ for } n\geq 3,
\end{equation}
where the bracket on the second line is the $\fg$-bracket composed
with the wedge product on the $(0,\bullet)$-Dolbeault factor.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[$L_\infty$ relations for $\cL_Y$;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{prop:6dhcs-linf-relations}
The system $(\cL_Y,\ell_1,\ell_2)$ of
Definition~\ref{def:6dhcs-gauge-linf} is an $L_\infty$-algebra: the
identities
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-linf-relations}
 \ell_1^2 \;=\; 0,
 \qquad
 \ell_1\ell_2 \;=\; \ell_2(\ell_1\otimes 1 + 1\otimes\ell_1),
 \qquad
 \sum_{\mathrm{cyc}} \ell_2(\ell_2\otimes 1) \;=\; 0,
\end{equation}
hold. All higher Jacobi identities reduce to
$\ell_3 = \ell_4 = \ldots = 0$ because the
action~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-classical-action}
is cubic and no higher multibrackets are generated.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
$\ell_1^2 = \bar\partial^2 = 0$ on $\Omega^{0,\bullet}(Y)$. The
Leibniz identity $\bar\partial[\alpha_1,\alpha_2] =
[\bar\partial\alpha_1,\alpha_2] + (-1)^{|\alpha_1|}[\alpha_1,\bar\partial
\alpha_2]$ is the second equation. The third equation is the graded
Jacobi identity on $\fg$ transported across the wedge product on
$\Omega^{0,\bullet}$; the sign rule is
$[\alpha_1,[\alpha_2,\alpha_3]] + (-1)^{|\alpha_1|(|\alpha_2|+|\alpha_3|)}
[\alpha_2,[\alpha_3,\alpha_1]] +
(-1)^{|\alpha_3|(|\alpha_1|+|\alpha_2|)}[\alpha_3,[\alpha_1,\alpha_2]] = 0$.
Higher brackets $\ell_{n\geq 3}$ would arise from quartic or higher
terms in the action; cubicity of~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-classical-action} kills them.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Chevalley--Eilenberg duality with classical
 observables; \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{prop:6dhcs-ce-dual}
\index{Chevalley--Eilenberg!dual of classical observables}
The classical observable factorisation algebra
$\mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:6dhcs-obs} is isomorphic to the
Chevalley--Eilenberg cochain complex of $\cL_Y$ shifted by $[1]$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-obs-is-ce}
 \mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}}(U)
 \;\cong\;
 \CE^\bullet\bigl(\cL_Y(U)[1]\bigr),
 \qquad
 \CE^\bullet(\cL[1])
 \;=\;
 \bigl(\Sym\bigl(\cL[1]^\vee\bigr),\;d_{\CE}\bigr),
\end{equation}
with $d_{\CE}$ the sum of $\ell_1^\vee$ and the Chevalley--Eilenberg
$\ell_2^\vee$-differential.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Costello--Gwilliam~\cite[Ch.~4,\S4.2]{CostelloGwilliam2017}
establishes for every cubic classical field theory an identification
$\mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}} \simeq \CE^\bullet(\cL[1])$ where $\cL$
is the $L_\infty$-algebra of linear BRST fields with bracket supplied
by the cubic interaction. Substituting
$\cL = \cL_Y = \Omega^{0,\bullet}(Y)\otimes\fg$ with
$\ell_1 = \bar\partial$ and $\ell_2 = [-,-]$
from~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-gauge-linf-brackets} produces the
identification~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-obs-is-ce}. The shift by $[1]$
accounts for the gauge-ghost convention: $\cL_Y^0 = \Omega^{0,0}
\otimes\fg$ holds the BRST ghost $c$ at cohomological degree $+1$
after $[1]$, and $\cL_Y^1 = \Omega^{0,1}\otimes\fg$ holds the field
$\cA$ at degree $0$ after $[1]$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Kodaira--Spencer and Maurer--Cartan}
\label{subsec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-ks}

\begin{theorem}[Maurer--Cartan locus as Kodaira--Spencer moduli;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{thm:6dhcs-mc-is-ks}
\index{Maurer--Cartan locus!Kodaira--Spencer}
\index{Kodaira--Spencer theorem!6d hCS avatar}
The Maurer--Cartan set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-mc-set}
 \MC(\cL_Y)
 \;=\;
 \Bigl\{\;\cA\in\cL_Y^1 \;\Bigm|\;
 \bar\partial\cA + \tfrac12[\cA,\cA] = 0\;\Bigr\}
\end{equation}
is the space of holomorphic deformations of the trivial $\fg$-valued
$(0,1)$-connection on $Y$. Two such elements are gauge-equivalent if
and only if they differ by the exponentiated action of
$\exp(\ell_0 + \ell_2(-,c))$ for $c\in\cL_Y^0$. The formal moduli
problem represented by $\cL_Y$ is the holomorphic Kodaira--Spencer
deformation functor of the pair $(Y,\fg)$ in the sense
of~\cite{KodairaSpencer1958,Tian1987,Todorov1989}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The Maurer--Cartan equation~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-mc-set} is the vanishing
of the Euler--Lagrange equation~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-el} for the action
$S_0 = \int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge\mathrm{CS}(\cA)$; this is
Proposition~\ref{prop:hcs-classical-master}. A solution
$\cA\in\Omega^{0,1}(Y)\otimes\fg$ with
$\bar\partial\cA + \tfrac12[\cA,\cA] = 0$ deforms the
$\bar\partial$-operator on the trivial $\fg$-bundle to
$\bar\partial + [\cA,-]$, which is integrable by the
Maurer--Cartan equation and therefore defines a new holomorphic
structure. Kodaira--Spencer~\cite{KodairaSpencer1958} and its
Calabi--Yau refinement by Tian~\cite{Tian1987} and
Todorov~\cite{Todorov1989} identify such deformations with the
formal moduli represented by the $L_\infty$-algebra
$\Omega^{0,\bullet}(Y,T_Y)$; replacing $T_Y$ by $\fg$ (the
gauge-bundle analogue) produces the displayed statement. Gauge
equivalence through $c\in\cL_Y^0$ implements the
Kodaira--Spencer action of infinitesimal diffeomorphisms of $Y$
lifted to the $\fg$-bundle.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Matching Tian--Todorov unobstructedness]
\label{rem:6dhcs-tian-todorov}
\index{Tian--Todorov unobstructedness}
On a Calabi--Yau three-fold $Y$ with $\fg$ abelian, the
Tian--Todorov lemma~\cite{Tian1987,Todorov1989} states that
Kodaira--Spencer deformations of complex structure are unobstructed.
For non-abelian $\fg$, the cubic Chern--Simons interaction produces a
genuine obstruction class in $H^2(\cL_Y,\cL_Y)$, computed in
\S\ref{subsec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-obs} below. The abelian case recovers
Tian--Todorov; the non-abelian case is the gauge-theoretic refinement.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The $(-1)$-shifted BV symplectic structure}
\label{subsec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-bv-symp}

\begin{definition}[Shifted cotangent of $\cL_Y$;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{def:6dhcs-shifted-cotangent}
\index{shifted cotangent!6d hCS BV}
The BV $L_\infty$-algebra of 6d hCS is the shifted cotangent of
$\cL_Y[1]$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-shifted-cotangent}
 \cE^{\BV}_Y
 \;=\;
 \cL_Y[1]\;\oplus\;\cL_Y^\vee[-2]
 \;=\;
 \Omega^{0,\bullet}(Y)\otimes\fg\;[1]
 \;\oplus\;
 \Omega^{3,\bullet}(Y)\otimes\fg^\vee\;[-2],
\end{equation}
where the Serre-dual identification
$(\Omega^{0,\bullet}(Y))^\vee\simeq\Omega^{3,3-\bullet}(Y)$ uses the
volume form $\Omega_Y$ and the invariant pairing
$\fg\otimes\fg^\vee\to\bC$. The four components correspond to
ghosts $c\in\Omega^{0,0}\otimes\fg$, fields
$\cA\in\Omega^{0,1}\otimes\fg$, antifields
$\cA^*\in\Omega^{3,2}\otimes\fg^\vee$, antighosts-of-ghosts
$c^*\in\Omega^{3,3}\otimes\fg^\vee$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[$(-1)$-shifted symplectic form;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{prop:6dhcs-shifted-symplectic}
\index{symplectic form!shifted, 6d hCS}
The pairing
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-bv-symplectic-form}
 \omega_{\BV}(\xi_1,\xi_2)
 \;=\;
 \int_Y \Omega_Y \wedge \langle \pi_{\cL}\xi_1,\;
 \pi_{\cL^\vee}\xi_2\rangle
 \;-\;
 \int_Y \Omega_Y \wedge \langle \pi_{\cL}\xi_2,\;
 \pi_{\cL^\vee}\xi_1\rangle,
\end{equation}
where $\pi_\cL,\pi_{\cL^\vee}$ project onto the two summands of
$\cE^{\BV}_Y$, is
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item closed: $d_{\mathrm{dR}}\omega_{\BV} = 0$ on $\cE^{\BV}_Y$;
\item non-degenerate: the induced map
$\cE^{\BV}_Y\to(\cE^{\BV}_Y)^\vee[-1]$ is an isomorphism of cochain
complexes;
\item of ghost degree $-1$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Closedness is automatic because
$\omega_{\BV}$ is constant-coefficient on the linear space
$\cE^{\BV}_Y$. Non-degeneracy is Serre duality on $Y$: for any
$\alpha\in\Omega^{0,p}(Y)\otimes\fg$, the pairing
$\int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge\langle\alpha,-\rangle$ identifies
$\alpha$ with an element of $(\Omega^{3,3-p}(Y)\otimes\fg^\vee)^\vee$
