and vice versa; the pairing is perfect because $\Omega_Y$ is
nowhere-vanishing. Ghost degree is checked directly: $\cL_Y[1]$
sits in ghost degrees $-1,0,1,2$ (corresponding to the four
Dolbeault pieces), $\cL_Y^\vee[-2]$ sits in ghost degrees
$-1,-2,-3,-4$, and the pairing of $\cL_Y[1]$ with $\cL_Y^\vee[-2]$
lands in ghost degree $-1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Explicit antifield pairing]
\label{rem:6dhcs-antifield-pairing}
The explicit pairings induced by $\omega_{\BV}$ are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-antifield-pairings}
 \langle c,\,c^*\rangle_{\BV}
 \;=\;
 \int_Y \Omega_Y\wedge\langle c,\,c^*\rangle,
 \qquad
 \langle\cA,\,\cA^*\rangle_{\BV}
 \;=\;
 \int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge\langle\cA,\,\cA^*\rangle,
\end{equation}
matching the antibracket~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-antibracket-formula}.
Ghosts pair with antighosts-of-ghosts and fields pair with
antifields; there are no cross terms because the Dolbeault form
degrees in the two summands of~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-shifted-cotangent}
match only diagonally.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Classical master equation as $L_\infty$ Maurer--Cartan}
\label{subsec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-cme-mc}

\begin{theorem}[Classical master equation is Maurer--Cartan on the
 BV $L_\infty$-algebra; \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{thm:6dhcs-cme-is-mc}
\index{classical master equation!Maurer--Cartan form}
The BV master action $S_{\BV}$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:6dhcs-bv-master-action} is a degree-zero element of
the Chevalley--Eilenberg algebra $\CE^\bullet(\cE^{\BV}_Y)$ and
satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-cme-mc}
 d_{\CE} S_{\BV} \;+\; \tfrac12\{S_{\BV},S_{\BV}\}_{\BV} \;=\; 0,
\end{equation}
the Maurer--Cartan equation on the $L_\infty$-algebra
$(\CE^\bullet(\cE^{\BV}_Y),\{-,-\}_{\BV})$. The three terms in the
expansion of $\{S_{\BV},S_{\BV}\}$ at the cubic level correspond,
respectively, to the Jacobi identity on $\fg$, the gauge-invariance
of $S_0$, and the closure of the gauge algebra.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Expand $S_{\BV}$ from~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-bv-master-action}:
\[
 S_{\BV} \;=\; S_0 + \int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge
 \langle\cA^*,\bar\partial c + [\cA,c]\rangle
 - \tfrac12\int_Y\Omega_Y\wedge\langle c^*,[c,c]\rangle.
\]
Compute $\{S_{\BV},S_{\BV}\}$ using the explicit
antibracket~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-antibracket-formula}. The
$0$-antifield piece vanishes by Lemma~\ref{lem:6dhcs-gauge}. The
$\cA^*$-linear piece collects two contributions: from
$\{S_0,\int\cA^*(\bar\partial c + [\cA,c])\}$, which gives the
gauge-invariance of $S_0$ tested on the BRST transformation
$\delta_c\cA$, and from the self-bracket of the second term, which
produces the Jacobi identity $[\bar\partial c + [\cA,c],c]$
vanishing mod $F^{0,2}(\cA)$-terms. The $c^*$-linear piece is
$\{(\int\cA^*[\cA,c]),(\int c^*[c,c])\}$, which evaluates to
$\int c^*[c,[c,c]]$ and vanishes by the Jacobi identity on
$\fg$ applied to $c$ (fermionic, so $[c,[c,c]] = 0$ is the
fermionic Jacobi statement). Summing:
$\tfrac12\{S_{\BV},S_{\BV}\} = 0$, which
is~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-cme-mc} because $d_{\CE}S_{\BV}$ computes
$\ell_1$-derivative terms that are already incorporated into the
antibracket through
Proposition~\ref{prop:6dhcs-ce-dual}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The Koszul--Tate resolution in the BV $L_\infty$ lane}
\label{subsec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-kt-linf}

\begin{theorem}[Koszul--Tate resolution from
 $L_\infty$-perspective; \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{thm:6dhcs-kt-linf}
\index{Koszul--Tate resolution!L-infinity interpretation}
The Koszul--Tate complex of
Proposition~\ref{prop:6dhcs-kt-resolution} is the
Chevalley--Eilenberg cochain complex of the BV $L_\infty$-algebra
$\cE^{\BV}_Y$ equipped with the total differential
$d_{\CE}^{\BV} = \delta_{\KT} + \delta_{\mathrm{BRST}}$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-kt-ce}
 \bigl(\CE^\bullet(\cE^{\BV}_Y),\;d_{\CE}^{\BV}\bigr)
 \;\simeq\;
 \bigl(\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(Y)\otimes\Lambda^\bullet
 [\cA^*,c^*]\otimes\fg^{\otimes\bullet},\;
 \delta_{\KT} + \delta_{\mathrm{BRST}}\bigr).
\end{equation}
The ghost-number-zero cohomology
$H^0(\CE^\bullet(\cE^{\BV}_Y),d_{\CE}^{\BV})$ is the algebra of
gauge-invariant functions on the on-shell locus
$\{F^{0,2}(\cA) = 0\}/\mathcal{G}$, where $\mathcal{G}$ is the group
of gauge transformations of Lemma~\ref{lem:6dhcs-gauge}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The Chevalley--Eilenberg complex of a $L_\infty$-algebra
$(\cL,\ell_1,\ell_2,\ldots)$ has differential
$d_{\CE}|_{\cL^\vee} = \ell_1^\vee + \ell_2^\vee + \ldots$; for
$\cE^{\BV}_Y$ the brackets~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-gauge-linf-brackets} extend
to the shifted cotangent by demanding compatibility with the
$(-1)$-shifted symplectic form of
Proposition~\ref{prop:6dhcs-shifted-symplectic}. The bracket
structure is then the Legendre dual, producing on cotangent
coordinates the Koszul--Tate pieces
$\delta_{\KT}\cA^* = F^{0,2}(\cA)$ and
$\delta_{\KT}c^* = \bar\partial\cA^* + [\cA,\cA^*]$
of~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-kt-differential}; the BRST pieces
$\delta_{\mathrm{BRST}}$ come from the $\ell_2^\vee$-differential on
the $c$-coordinates. Acyclicity in positive antifield degree is
Proposition~\ref{prop:6dhcs-kt-resolution}; the cohomology statement
in ghost degree $0$ is classical Henneaux--Teitelboim
homological reduction~\cite[\S10.2,\S10.4]{HenneauxTeitelboim1992}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Infinitesimal deformations and the Atiyah class}
\label{subsec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-def}

\begin{theorem}[Deformations of 6d hCS classified by $H^1$;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{thm:6dhcs-h1}
\index{deformations!6d hCS, H1}
Infinitesimal deformations of the classical theory
$(\cL_Y,\ell_1,\ell_2)$ within the class of cubic holomorphic field
theories are classified by the first Chevalley--Eilenberg cohomology
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-def-h1}
 \mathrm{Def}^1(\cL_Y) \;=\; H^1\bigl(\cL_Y,\cL_Y\bigr)
 \;=\; H^1\bigl(\CE^\bullet(\cL_Y,\cL_Y),\;d_{\CE}\bigr),
\end{equation}
where $\CE^\bullet(\cL_Y,\cL_Y)$ is the Chevalley--Eilenberg complex
with coefficients in the adjoint representation. Equivalently, on the
Calabi--Yau three-fold $Y$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-def-dolbeault}
 \mathrm{Def}^1(\cL_Y)
 \;\cong\;
 H^1\bigl(Y,\;\mathrm{End}(\fg\otimes\cO_Y)\bigr),
\end{equation}
the first Dolbeault cohomology of the endomorphism bundle of the
trivial $\fg$-bundle on $Y$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
A one-parameter deformation of the $L_\infty$ structure
$(\ell_1,\ell_2)$ is a Maurer--Cartan element of the adjoint
$L_\infty$-algebra $\CE^\bullet(\cL_Y,\cL_Y)$, i.e.\ a
$\CE^\bullet(\cL_Y)$-derivation of degree $+1$ squaring to zero modulo
gauge. Such a derivation is determined by its action on linear
generators $\cL_Y^\vee$; the degree-$+1$ derivations squaring to
zero modulo exact derivations are
$H^1(\CE^\bullet(\cL_Y,\cL_Y),d_{\CE}) = H^1(\cL_Y,\cL_Y)$, which is
the first equation. Decomposing $\cL_Y = \Omega^{0,\bullet}(Y)\otimes
\fg$ and unfolding the Chevalley--Eilenberg complex as an adjoint
Dolbeault complex yields
$H^1(\cL_Y,\cL_Y) \simeq H^1(Y,\mathrm{End}(\fg\otimes\cO_Y))$,
the second equation.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Deformations for $Y = \R^3\times K3\times\C^2$;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{cor:6dhcs-def-r3-k3-c2}
\index{deformations!K3 Heisenberg setup}
For $Y = \R^3\times K3\times\C^2$ and $\fg$ supporting a sheaf $E$ of
holomorphic $\fg$-bundles over $K3$, the Künneth decomposition gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-def-k3-kunneth}
 H^1(\cL_Y,\cL_Y)
 \;\simeq\;
 \bigoplus_{p+q+r = 1}
 H^p_{\mathrm{dR}}(\R^3)
 \otimes H^q(K3,\mathrm{End}\,E)
 \otimes H^r(\C^2,\cO_{\C^2}).
\end{equation}
The $\R^3$ factor contributes only $p = 0$ (de~Rham-trivial by
Poincar\'{e}); the $\C^2$ factor contributes only $r = 0$ (affine
Dolbeault-trivial); the deformations are therefore concentrated at
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-def-k3-endE}
 H^1(\cL_Y,\cL_Y) \;\simeq\; H^1\bigl(K3,\mathrm{End}\,E\bigr).
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Künneth for Dolbeault cohomology on a product of complex manifolds
(extended across the real factor $\R^3$ by the de~Rham--Dolbeault
reduction on $\cO$-coefficients) yields~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-def-k3-kunneth}.
On $\R^3$, Poincar\'{e} lemma gives
$H^p_{\mathrm{dR}}(\R^3) = 0$ for $p > 0$. On $\C^2$, the Dolbeault
cohomology of $\cO_{\C^2}$ with compact support vanishes except at
$p = 2$; in the relevant \emph{non-compact} category of polynomial
growth, the translation-invariant observables pick out the $r = 0$
piece only (Costello--Francis--Gwilliam~\cite[\S5]{CFG2026}
localisation to $z = 0$). This reduces~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-def-k3-kunneth}
to~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-def-k3-endE}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Atiyah class obstruction]
\label{rem:6dhcs-atiyah}
\index{Atiyah class!6d hCS obstruction}
The second-order obstruction to a first-order deformation
$\alpha\in H^1(K3,\mathrm{End}\,E)$ is $\alpha\cup\alpha \in
H^2(K3,\mathrm{End}\,E)$, the cup product of the first-order class
with itself. By the Atiyah class interpretation
$\mathrm{At}(E)\in H^1(K3,\Omega^1\otimes\mathrm{End}\,E)$, the
obstruction
$\alpha\cup\alpha = \mathrm{tr}(\mathrm{At}(E)\cdot\alpha\cdot\alpha)
\in H^2(K3,\mathrm{End}\,E)$ vanishes identically when
$\mathrm{At}(E) = 0$ (flat bundle), and non-trivially when $E$ has
non-zero Atiyah class.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Obstruction theory and the $H^2$ class}
\label{subsec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-obs}

\begin{theorem}[Obstruction sequence for order-$n$ deformations;
 \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{thm:6dhcs-obs-sequence}
\index{obstruction theory!L-infinity}
Let $\cL_Y^{(n)}$ be an order-$n$ deformation of $\cL_Y$, i.e.\ a
Maurer--Cartan element in $\CE^\bullet(\cL_Y,\cL_Y)[\![t]\!]/(t^{n+1})$.
The obstruction to lifting $\cL_Y^{(n)}$ to
$\cL_Y^{(n+1)}$ is a cohomology class
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-obs-class}
 \mathrm{Obs}^{n+1}\bigl(\cL_Y^{(n)}\bigr)
 \;\in\;
 H^2\bigl(\cL_Y,\cL_Y\bigr).
\end{equation}
The lift exists if and only if
$\mathrm{Obs}^{n+1}(\cL_Y^{(n)}) = 0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Deligne--Goldman--Millson deformation
theory~\cite[Ch.~4]{CostelloGwilliam2017}: the obstruction to
extending a $L_\infty$-Maurer--Cartan series $\cA_1 t +
\cA_2 t^2 + \ldots + \cA_n t^n$ to order $n+1$ is
$\tfrac12\sum_{i+j = n+1}\ell_2(\cA_i,\cA_j) \pmod{\mathrm{image\
of\ }\ell_1}$, which is a cocycle in
$H^2(\cL_Y,\cL_Y)$ and is exact if and only if the lift exists.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Obstruction for $Y = \R^3\times K3\times\C^2$
 with $24$ $D0$-branes; \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{cor:6dhcs-obs-k3}
\index{D0-branes!24, K3 anomaly}
For the twisted six-dimensional hCS setup with bundle $E$ supported on
$24$ $D0$-brane locations on $K3$ (mirror to the $24$ $I_1$ fibres of
an elliptic K3), the obstruction class lives in
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6dhcs-obs-k3-target}
 \mathrm{Obs}^{n+1} \;\in\; H^2\bigl(K3,\mathrm{End}\,E\bigr)
 \;\simeq\;
 \bigoplus_{i = 1}^{24}\bigl(\End_{\fg}\bigr),
\end{equation}
where the sum ranges over the $24$ $D0$-brane loci and each summand
is a copy of the $\fg$-endomorphism algebra localised at a single
brane. Writing
\(\mathrm{Obs}^{n+1}_i\in\End_\fg\) for the local class at the
\(i\)-th support point, the global lift exists if and only if
\[
 \sum_{i=1}^{24}\mathrm{Obs}^{n+1}_i=0
 \quad\text{in }H^2(K3,\mathrm{End}\,E).
\]
The Yau--Zaslow count supplies the number of nodal fibres; it does not
by itself force this sum to vanish.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The K\"unneth reduction
of Corollary~\ref{cor:6dhcs-def-r3-k3-c2} applied at degree $2$
yields $H^2(\cL_Y,\cL_Y) \simeq H^2(K3,\mathrm{End}\,E)$ because
$H^p_{\mathrm{dR}}(\R^3) = 0$ for $p > 0$ and the $\C^2$-factor
contributes only at cohomological degree $0$ in the Dolbeault
complex under translation invariance. For $E$ supported on $24$
$D0$-brane loci, Serre duality and Grothendieck--Verdier
localisation yield the decomposition~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-obs-k3-target};
the right-hand side is the $24$-fold direct sum of local
$\End_\fg$ summands, and the global obstruction vanishes iff the
total sum is zero. The Yau--Zaslow count identifies the geometric
source of the \(24\) summands; Bogomolov--Mukai stability is a
separate condition on \(E\), not a vanishing theorem for
\(\mathrm{Obs}^{n+1}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[BKM comparison target and upgrade criterion]
\label{rem:6dhcs-bkm-link}
\index{Borcherds--Kac--Moody!6d hCS origin}
Corollary~\ref{cor:6dhcs-obs-k3} proves a $24$-fold local
BV/hCS obstruction target. After choosing a trace, or after equipping
the local endomorphism algebra with a parity and choosing a supertrace,
the direct sum in~\eqref{eq:6dhcs-obs-k3-target} can furnish a scalar
obstruction coefficient. This supplies an obstruction pattern compatible
with the \(K3\) Euler number appearing in the Igusa/Borcherds
normalisation. It does not, without further data, identify the local
summands with root spaces of the Borcherds--Kac--Moody algebra
\(\fg_{\Delta_5}\), nor does it construct the Vol III BKM chiral
bialgebra \(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}\).

The upgrade to a BKM chain-recognition theorem requires the following
data. One must construct:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.8em]
\item a source BV/hCS dg Lie or \(L_\infty\)-algebra and a chain map
from its local obstruction complex to the Chevalley--Eilenberg complex
of the proposed BKM or BKM chiral algebra;
\item a parity on the local summands, so that the comparison uses
superdimensions rather than plain dimensions;
\item root labels \(\alpha(n,\ell)\) placing each contributing local
class in the same positive cone and Jacobi-coefficient convention as
the Borcherds product for \(\Delta_5\);
\item equality of the resulting graded supertrace with the
denominator-identity exponents \(c(n,\ell)\), including the Weyl vector
and sign conventions;
\item a normalisation comparison fixing \(\Delta_5\) versus
\(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\), the two separating \(\eta^{24}\) factors, and
the trace pairing on \(\End\,E\).
\end{enumerate}
Only after these five data are supplied may the local BV/hCS obstruction
complex be identified with the BKM root-space chain model underlying
\(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}\). Until then, the statement is a scalar or
supertrace comparison target, not a proved root-multiplicity
identification.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Side-by-side with Costello--Francis--Gwilliam 2026}
\label{subsec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-cfg}

\begin{remark}[Dictionary table]
\label{rem:6dhcs-cfg-dictionary}
\index{Costello--Francis--Gwilliam!2026, dictionary}
The construction of \S\ref{subsec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-linf}--%
\ref{subsec:vol2-bvbrst-6dhcs-obs} follows Costello--Francis--Gwilliam
2026~\cite{CFG2026} step-by-step, with each 6d-hCS step being the
holomorphic avatar of the corresponding step in their treatment of
the $E_3$-algebra of observables.

{\small
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lll}
\toprule
\emph{CFG 2026 step} & \emph{Our 6d hCS step} & \emph{Difference} \\
\midrule
Linear BV $\Linf$-algebra & $\cL_Y = \Omega^{0,\bullet}(Y)\otimes\fg$
 & none ($K_Y$ trivial) \\
Brackets $\ell_1,\ell_2$ from cubic action
 & $\ell_1 = \bar\partial$, $\ell_2 = [-,-]$ & none \\
$\CE^\bullet(\cL[1]) \simeq \Obs^{\mathrm{cl}}$
 & Prop.~\ref{prop:6dhcs-ce-dual} & none \\
$\MC(\cL)$ as formal moduli
 & Thm.~\ref{thm:6dhcs-mc-is-ks}, Kodaira--Spencer & none \\
$(-1)$-shifted cotangent $T^*[-1]\cL[1]$
 & Def.~\ref{def:6dhcs-shifted-cotangent} & none (Serre duality used) \\
Symplectic form $\omega_{\BV}$ & Prop.~\ref{prop:6dhcs-shifted-symplectic}
 & none \\
CME as $\Linf$-MC of $\cE^{\BV}$
 & Thm.~\ref{thm:6dhcs-cme-is-mc} & none \\
Koszul--Tate as $\CE^\bullet(\cE^{\BV})$
 & Thm.~\ref{thm:6dhcs-kt-linf} & none \\
$H^1(\cL,\cL)$ classifies deformations
 & Thm.~\ref{thm:6dhcs-h1} & none \\
$H^2(\cL,\cL)$ obstructs lift
 & Thm.~\ref{thm:6dhcs-obs-sequence} & none \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
}

Every row is an equivalence; the ``Difference'' column is empty at
every level because the Calabi--Yau condition $K_Y\simeq\cO_Y$
trivialises the volume form and reduces the general CFG argument to
the specific 6d hCS form presented here.
\end{remark}
